python3 -m pytest tests/unit/grip_contact/test_mujoco_grip_lab_adapter.py
python3 -m pytest tests/unit/grip_contact/test_grip_binding_manifests.py
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{MyoHand Articulated Bilateral Grip Fixture (HRG-GS-M8, \#13330)}
\label{sec:hrg-gs-m8-myohand-bilateral}

Issue~\#13330 (\texttt{HRG-GS-M8}) composes the pinned MyoHub \texttt{myoHand} right-hand chain with a sagittally mirrored left \texttt{myoHand} chain and a 6-DOF free golf club (\texttt{driver} or \texttt{iron7}) into an articulated bilateral grip fixture (\texttt{src/engines/physics\_engines/myosuite/python/myohand\_bilateral\_scene.py}, \texttt{myohand\_bilateral\_adapter.py}, and \texttt{myohand\_grip\_controller.py}), binding all $15$ shared \texttt{ContactTopology} sites onto distinct phalanx, metacarpal, and thumb segments (\texttt{representation="phalange"}, \texttt{attachment\_mode="separated\_v2"}).

\textbf{Scene Composition and Asset Hash Provenance.}
The pure-function scene generator \texttt{generate\_myohand\_bilateral\_scene} reads \texttt{shared/models/myosuite/myo\_sim/hand/myohand.xml}, \texttt{hand/assets/myohand\_assets.xml}, and \texttt{hand/assets/myohand\_body.xml} at the pinned commit \texttt{33f3ded946f55adbdcf963c99999587aadaf975f} and writes two standalone MJCF scenes under \texttt{shared/models/myosuite/golf/hand/}:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \texttt{myohand\_bilateral\_driver.xml} ($m_{\mathrm{club}} = 0.313\,\mathrm{kg}$, $L = 1.156\,\mathrm{m}$, SHA-256 \texttt{b374ae8337c4cc38e35c51e7fa112c6a6dc1172dab7c601c80656f10c62bb13d});
  \item \texttt{myohand\_bilateral\_iron.xml} ($m_{\mathrm{club}} = 0.428\,\mathrm{kg}$, $L = 0.940\,\mathrm{m}$, SHA-256 \texttt{dae0ec8e8e08872144d14cce5516df1ed7f6001e22648cd01e714bac52649677});
  \item \texttt{assets/myohand\_right\_chain.xml} (SHA-256 \texttt{348b88dbcd7e15d5bebc0f55f070cda73b5e4d3bde090234fa12db9049e1ffc6}) and \texttt{assets/myohand\_left\_chain.xml} (SHA-256 \texttt{de5ef817d691e56f2d9605fab9eedcd6bfc9791bf98de6876cc7e0650c17e08b}).
\end{itemize}
All visual and collision STL meshes are referenced via relative \texttt{meshdir="../../myo\_sim"} paths without copying any \texttt{myo\_sim} assets. The committed receipt \texttt{shared/models/myosuite/golf/hand/myohand\_bilateral\_receipt.json} records the pin commit and the upstream XML SHA-256 digests (\texttt{myohand.xml}: \texttt{f744d0d3\ldots c0c1}; \texttt{myohand\_assets.xml}: \texttt{2d6bd1f8\ldots 3dc8}; \texttt{myohand\_body.xml}: \texttt{4b55b3fb\ldots 6b4862}; \texttt{myosuite\_scene.xml}: \texttt{39e342fc\ldots 4e11a}).

\textbf{Left-Hand Sagittal Mirroring Outcome.}
Because \texttt{myohand.xml} provides only a right hand ($37$ bodies from \texttt{thorax} to \texttt{distph5}, $23$ wrist and finger joints), \texttt{generate\_myohand\_bilateral\_scene} constructs the left-hand kinematic chain (\texttt{lh\_*}) via coupled world-sagittal reflection $\mathbf{S}_W = \operatorname{diag}(-1, 1, 1)$ and body-local reflection $\mathbf{S}_B = \operatorname{diag}(1, 1, -1)$ with mirrored mesh scale $\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{mesh}} = (1, 1, -1)$:
\begin{equation}
  \mathbf{R}_{0,L} = \mathbf{S}_W \mathbf{R}_{0,R} \mathbf{S}_B, \qquad
  \mathbf{R}_{p\to c,L} = \mathbf{S}_B \mathbf{R}_{p\to c,R} \mathbf{S}_B, \qquad
  \mathbf{p}_{c,L} = \mathbf{S}_B \mathbf{p}_{c,R}, \qquad
  \mathbf{a}_{j,L} = -\mathbf{S}_B \mathbf{a}_{j,R}.
\end{equation}
Since $\det(\mathbf{S}_W)\det(\mathbf{S}_B) = (-1)(-1) = +1$, every body frame $\mathbf{R}_{B,L} \in \mathrm{SO}(3)$ remains a proper right-handed rotation, while the world inertia tensor transforms as $\mathbf{I}_{W,L}(q) = \mathbf{S}_W \mathbf{I}_{W,R}(q) \mathbf{S}_W$ and the axial jointvector conjugation $-\mathbf{S}_B \mathbf{a}_{j,R}$ preserves identical joint-angle signs $q_L = q_R$ for flexion and abduction. Across all $37$ bodies and $23$ joints in both neutral and articulated poses (\texttt{verify\_left\_hand\_sagittal\_reflection}), the measured reflection residuals are:
\begin{itemize}
  \item Neutral and articulated body origin error: $\max_b \|\mathbf{S}_W \mathbf{x}_{b,R} - \mathbf{x}_{b,L}\|_2 = 1.67 \times 10^{-16}\,\mathrm{m}$ (neutral) and $1.11 \times 10^{-16}\,\mathrm{m}$ (articulated);
  \item Neutral and articulated COM position error: $\max_b \|\mathbf{S}_W \mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{com},b,R} - \mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{com},b,L}\|_2 = 1.67 \times 10^{-16}\,\mathrm{m}$ (neutral) and $1.11 \times 10^{-16}\,\mathrm{m}$ (articulated);
  \item Proper rotation error: $\max_b \|\mathbf{S}_W \mathbf{R}_{b,R} \mathbf{S}_B - \mathbf{R}_{b,L}\|_F = 7.19 \times 10^{-16}$;
  \item Body mass and world inertia tensor error: $\max_b |m_{b,R} - m_{b,L}| = 0.0\,\mathrm{kg}$ and $\max_b \|\mathbf{S}_W \mathbf{I}_{W,b,R} \mathbf{S}_W - \mathbf{I}_{W,b,L}\|_F = 2.43 \times 10^{-16}\,\mathrm{kg\cdot m^2}$;
  \item Reflected palmar contact normal error: $\max_b \|\mathbf{S}_W \mathbf{n}_{b,R} - \mathbf{n}_{b,L}\|_2 = 3.68 \times 10^{-16}$.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Site-to-Segment and Preset-to-Joint Mapping Tables.}
The manifests \texttt{src/config/grip\_bindings/myosuite\_myohand\_bilateral\_driver.json} and \texttt{myosuite\_myohand\_bilateral\_iron.json} map $100\%$ of requested \texttt{ContactTopology} sites across \texttt{overlap}, \texttt{interlock}, and \texttt{ten\_finger} (with and without \texttt{trigger\_finger}) onto $15$ distinct anatomical segments (\texttt{unavailable\_sites = \{\}}), summarized in Table~\ref{tab:hrg-gs-m8-segments}. Table~\ref{tab:hrg-gs-m8-joints} documents \texttt{FINGER\_POSE\_JOINT\_MAP} (\texttt{pose\_to\_myohand\_joint\_targets}), which maps any \texttt{GripPose} (\texttt{src/shared/python/model\_appearance/grip\_pose.py}) and \texttt{HandMeshParams} (\texttt{grip\_hand\_mesh.py}) onto the $23$ \texttt{myoHand} joint targets per hand.

\textbf{Equations and Parameters.}
For each finger $k \in \{2,3,4,5\}$ with phalanx lengths $\ell_{k,1}, \ell_{k,2}, \ell_{k,3}$ scaled by $s = \texttt{params.scale}$ around effective cylinder radius $r_{\mathrm{eff}} = r_{\mathrm{grip}} + c_{\mathrm{pad}}$ ($r_{\mathrm{grip}} = 0.014\,\mathrm{m}$, $c_{\mathrm{pad}} = 0.002\,\mathrm{m}$), the wrap angles are $\theta_{\mathrm{mcp},k} = 0.65\,s\ell_{k,1}/r_{\mathrm{eff}}$, $\theta_{\mathrm{pip},k} = 0.85\,s\ell_{k,2}/r_{\mathrm{eff}}$, and $\theta_{\mathrm{dip},k} = 0.70\,s\ell_{k,3}/r_{\mathrm{eff}}$, clamped to the anatomical joint limits of \texttt{myohand\_body.xml}. In frozen-finger acceptance mode (\texttt{frozen\_fingers=True}), \texttt{MyoHandBilateralGripAdapter} writes $q_j = q_j^{\star}$ into \texttt{data.qpos}, activates the $46$ MuJoCo \texttt{<joint>} equality constraints (\texttt{eq\_active = 1}, polynomial target $q_j^{\star}$), and zeroes all $78$ muscle controls and activations (\texttt{data.ctrl = 0}, \texttt{data.act = 0}). In \texttt{contact\_mode="shared"}, all geom collision masks are zeroed (\texttt{geom\_contype = 0}, \texttt{geom\_conaffinity = 0}) so native MuJoCo contact is strictly excluded (\texttt{registered\_grip\_collision\_count() == 0}), and COM-transferred wrenches from both hand--club and physical hand--hand pairs (\texttt{trail\_palm\_lead\_thumb}, \texttt{overlap\_little\_finger\_lead\_index}, \texttt{interlock\_little\_finger\_lead\_middle}, \texttt{interlock\_lead\_index\_wrap}) are applied to \texttt{data.xfrc\_applied}.

\textbf{Club Mass/Inertia Provenance, Independent MuJoCo \texttt{mj\_inverse} Requirement, and Bimanual Grasp Map.}
In the initial M8 commit (\texttt{96405cce03}), \texttt{DRIVER\_CLUB\_PROPERTIES} and \texttt{IRON\_CLUB\_PROPERTIES} in \texttt{myohand\_grip\_controller.py} were hand-entered constants copied and rounded to $8$ decimal places on the inertia diagonal ($\operatorname{diag}(0.03744414, 0.00024399, 0.0374877)\,\mathrm{kg\cdot m^2}$ driver; $\operatorname{diag}(0.0343317, 0.0001, 0.03428158)\,\mathrm{kg\cdot m^2}$ 7-iron), which introduced a $3.063 \times 10^{-8}$ peak / $2.217 \times 10^{-8}$ RMS (driver) and $5.497 \times 10^{-8}$ peak / $3.979 \times 10^{-8}$ RMS (7-iron) moment rounding discrepancy against the compiled MuJoCo scene. \texttt{ClubInertialProperties.from\_club\_spec(club)} now derives $(m_{\mathrm{club}}, \mathbf{r}_{\mathrm{com}}, \mathbf{I}_{\mathrm{com}})$ in closed form from the \texttt{ClubSpec} solid geometry (\texttt{CLUBS["driver"]} and \texttt{CLUBS["iron7"]} in \path{src/shared/python/motion_matching/club_models.py}: uniform capsule shaft+grip of length $L$, radius $r_s$, mass $m_s = m_{\mathrm{shaft}} + m_{\mathrm{grip}}$ plus ellipsoid/box head of half-sizes $(h_x, h_y, h_z)$ and mass $m_h$ via the parallel-axis theorem), completely independent of \texttt{MjModel}/\texttt{MjData}. Comparing \texttt{from\_club\_spec} against MuJoCo's \texttt{<compiler inertiafromgeom="auto" balanceinertia="true" boundmass="0.001" boundinertia=".0001"/>} (\texttt{adapter.club\_scene\_inertial\_properties()}, which rotates \texttt{model.body\_inertia} by \texttt{model.body\_iquat} back into the club body frame) shows exact agreement on mass ($0.313\,\mathrm{kg}$ driver, $0.428\,\mathrm{kg}$ 7-iron), COM offset ($y_{\mathrm{com}} = -0.21236421725239618\,\mathrm{m}$ driver, $-0.17570093457943925\,\mathrm{m}$ 7-iron), and transverse inertias $I_{xx}, I_{zz}$ to $\le 1.39 \times 10^{-17}\,\mathrm{kg\cdot m^2}$, while for the 7-iron MuJoCo's \texttt{boundinertia=".0001"} compiler floor clamps the axial shaft moment from $I_{yy,\mathrm{spec}} = 6.492449 \times 10^{-5}\,\mathrm{kg\cdot m^2}$ up to $I_{yy,\mathrm{mj}} = 1.000000 \times 10^{-4}\,\mathrm{kg\cdot m^2}$.

Over the $21$-step ($\Delta t = 0.002\,\mathrm{s}$, $T = 0.04\,\mathrm{s}$, $\Omega = 2\pi/T = 50\pi \approx 157.080\,\mathrm{rad/s}$) kinematically consistent prescribed club trajectory
\begin{equation}
  \mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{origin}}(t) = \int_0^t \mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{origin}}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau = \begin{bmatrix} \frac{0.02}{\Omega}\sin(\Omega t) \\ 0 \\ 0 \end{bmatrix}\mathrm{m}, \qquad
  \mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{club}}(t) = \mathbf{R}_z\!\bigl(\theta_z(t)\bigr), \qquad
  \theta_z(t) = \int_0^t \omega_z(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau = \frac{0.15}{\Omega}\bigl(1 - \cos(\Omega t)\bigr)\,\mathrm{rad},
\end{equation}
with $\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{origin}}(t) = [0.02\cos(\Omega t), 0, 0]^{\top}\,\mathrm{m/s}$, $\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathrm{club}}(t) = [0, 0, 0.15\sin(\Omega t)]^{\top}\,\mathrm{rad/s}$, $\mathbf{a}_{\mathrm{origin}}(t) = [-0.02\,\Omega\sin(\Omega t), 0, 0]^{\top}\,\mathrm{m/s^2}$, and $\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\mathrm{club}}(t) = [0, 0, 0.15\,\Omega\cos(\Omega t)]^{\top}\,\mathrm{rad/s^2}$, \texttt{evaluate\_grasp\_resultant\_newton\_check} evaluates two independent inverse-dynamics requirements:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,noitemsep]
  \item \textit{Independent MuJoCo \texttt{mj\_inverse} Requirement (\texttt{club\_mj\_inverse\_requirement}):} At each step $t_k$, sets \texttt{club\_free} \texttt{qpos} and \texttt{qvel} from the prescribed trajectory and \texttt{qacc} from either (a) the analytic second derivative or (b) second-order central differences of the prescribed \texttt{qvel} (\texttt{np.gradient(qvel\_seq, dt\_s, axis=0, edge\_order=2)}); zeroes \texttt{data.xfrc\_applied} and \texttt{data.ctrl}; runs \texttt{mujoco.mj\_inverse}; verifies zero club constraint/contact force ($\|\mathbf{qfrc}_{\mathrm{constraint,club}}\|_2 = 0.0\,\mathrm{N}$); and converts \texttt{qfrc\_inverse} on the $6$ \texttt{club\_free} DOFs (where MuJoCo's free joint expresses linear DOFs $\mathbf{qfrc}_{\mathrm{inv},0:3}$ in world axes at \texttt{xpos} and angular DOFs $\mathbf{qfrc}_{\mathrm{inv},3:6}$ in body-local axes about \texttt{xpos}) to world force and moment about the club COM (\texttt{xipos}):
  \begin{equation}
    \mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{mj},W} = \mathbf{qfrc}_{\mathrm{inv},0:3}, \qquad
    \mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{mj,origin},W} = \mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{club}}\,\mathbf{qfrc}_{\mathrm{inv},3:6}, \qquad
    \mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{mj,com},W} = \mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{mj,origin},W} + (\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{origin}} - \mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{com}}) \times \mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{mj},W}.
  \end{equation}
  This coordinate conversion is verified to $\le 1.0 \times 10^{-13}$ on a general 3D pose, velocity, and acceleration (\texttt{test\_mujoco\_free\_joint\_mj\_inverse\_frame\_conversion\_convention}), and non-circularity is verified by scaling applied hand wrenches by $\times 2$, which leaves both \texttt{mj\_inverse} and analytic requirements invariant while doubling the hand wrench error (\texttt{test\_club\_inverse\_dynamics\_requirement\_is\_non\_circular\_and\_sliding\_cone\_ratios}).
  \item \textit{Analytic \texttt{ClubSpec} Requirement (\texttt{club\_inverse\_dynamics\_requirement}):} Evaluates $\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{req}}(t) = m_{\mathrm{club}}(\mathbf{a}_{\mathrm{com}}(t) - \mathbf{g})$ and $\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{req,com}}(t) = \mathbf{I}_{\mathrm{com}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\mathrm{club}}(t) + \boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathrm{club}}(t) \times (\mathbf{I}_{\mathrm{com}}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathrm{club}}(t))$ using \texttt{ClubInertialProperties.from\_club\_spec}. The discrepancy between the analytic requirement and the MuJoCo \texttt{mj\_inverse} requirement (analytic \texttt{qacc}) is $1.24 \times 10^{-16}$ peak / $5.95 \times 10^{-17}$ RMS on force and $5.28 \times 10^{-15}$ peak / $3.79 \times 10^{-15}$ RMS on moment (driver), and $0.00$ peak / $5.09 \times 10^{-17}$ RMS on force and $2.89 \times 10^{-15}$ peak / $2.13 \times 10^{-15}$ RMS on moment (7-iron).
\end{enumerate}
Let $\mathbf{y} = [\mathbf{w}_L^{\top}, \mathbf{w}_R^{\top}]^{\top} \in \mathbb{R}^{12}$ denote the stacked lead- and trail-hand wrenches about reference points $\mathbf{r}_L, \mathbf{r}_R$ on the grip axis ($r_{x,L} = +0.024\,\mathrm{m}$, $r_{x,R} = -0.0522\,\mathrm{m}$, axial separation $d_x = 76.2\,\mathrm{mm}$). The bimanual grasp map $\mathbf{G} = [\mathbf{T}_L\;\mathbf{T}_R] \in \mathbb{R}^{6 \times 12}$ maps $\mathbf{y}$ onto the net club wrench $\mathbf{w}_{\mathrm{club}} = \mathbf{G}\mathbf{y}$ about $\mathbf{r}_{\mathrm{mid}} = \frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{r}_L + \mathbf{r}_R)$ with $\dim\ker(\mathbf{G}) = 6$. In accordance with \S 19, any solution takes the form $\mathbf{y} = \mathbf{G}^{+}\mathbf{w}_{\mathrm{club}} + \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}$, where $\mathbf{K} \in \mathbb{R}^{12 \times 6}$ spans the internal nullspace and $\mathbf{s} = \boldsymbol{\Phi}\mathbf{y} = \frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{w}_R - \mathbf{w}_L) \in \mathbb{R}^6$ is the mechanically indeterminate internal wrench split (\texttt{per\_hand\_split\_status = "INDETERMINATE"}, \texttt{squeeze\_status = "INDETERMINATE"}).

\textbf{Force-Regulated Bimanual Grip Controller, Solver-Closure Residual, and Open-Loop Forward Replay.}
In the prior open-loop sliding baseline, \texttt{build\_prescribed\_grip\_trajectory} applied an unregulated unidirectional shift $\Delta \mathbf{p}(t) = [0.4\sin(\Omega t), -1.5 - 0.3\cos(\Omega t), 0.2\sin(\Omega t)]^{\top}\,\mathrm{mm}$ and slip velocity $\Delta \mathbf{v}(t) = [15\cos(\Omega t), 10\sin(\Omega t), 5\cos(\Omega t)]^{\top}\,\mathrm{mm/s}$. That shift unloaded all $5$ palm/heel pads on the $-y_{\mathrm{grip}}$ side ($f_n = 0\,\mathrm{N}$) and compressed only the $5$ (\texttt{overlap}/\texttt{interlock}) or $6$ (\texttt{ten\_finger}) finger pads on the $+y_{\mathrm{grip}}$ side to $f_n(T) = 350.745\,\mathrm{N}$ at tangential slip speed $\|\mathbf{v}_t(T)\| = 15.811\,\mathrm{mm/s} = 15.811\,v_{\mathrm{tr}}$ ($v_{\mathrm{tr}} = 1.0\,\mathrm{mm/s}$), saturating every finger pad at dynamic sliding friction $\|\mathbf{f}_t\|/(\mu_s f_n) = \mu_d/\mu_s = 0.70/0.90 = 0.7778$ and producing a net club force $\|\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{hand}}(T)\| = 2140.70\,\mathrm{N}$ (\texttt{overlap}/\texttt{interlock}) or $2568.84\,\mathrm{N}$ (\texttt{ten\_finger}) against the $\le 4.557\,\mathrm{N}$ club requirement ($384\times$--$716\times$ force error and $1328\times$--$2182\times$ moment error).

To regulate the net hand wrench without artificial force scaling, \texttt{myohand\_grip\_controller.py} (\texttt{solve\_force\_regulated\_hand\_frames}) places both hands in a bilateral preload squeeze around $\delta_0 = 0.25\,\mathrm{mm}$ ($f_{n,0} = k_n \delta_0 = 42.774\,\mathrm{N}$ per pad at $k_n = 171\,095\,\mathrm{N/m}$, $c_n = 70.0\,\mathrm{N\cdot s/m}$) so that the $5$ palm/heel pads ($\hat{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathrm{grip}} = [0, +1, 0]^{\top}$) and the $5$ or $6$ finger/trigger pads ($\hat{\mathbf{n}}_{\mathrm{grip}} = [0, -1, 0]^{\top}$) compress the grip cylinder from opposite sides. Keeping both hand frames rotationally locked to the club grip frame ($\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{hand},s}(t) = \mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{club},s}(t)$), the controller solves at each step $s \in \{0, \ldots, 20\}$ for the $6$-DOF bimanual regulation vector in the club grip frame:
\begin{equation}
  \mathbf{q}(t_s) = \bigl(\Delta p_{y,L},\; \Delta p_{y,R},\; \Delta v_x,\; \Delta v_{z,L},\; \Delta v_{z,R},\; \Delta \omega_x\bigr)^{\top} \in \mathbb{R}^6, \qquad
  \mathbf{G}\mathbf{y}\bigl(\mathbf{q}(t_s)\bigr) = \mathbf{w}_{\mathrm{req,mid}}(t_s).
\end{equation}
Here $(\Delta p_{y,L}, \Delta p_{y,R})$ modulate the lead and trail normal-force differentials around the $\delta_0 = 0.25\,\mathrm{mm}$ squeeze to satisfy $F_{y,L} + F_{y,R} = F_{\mathrm{req},y}$ and the yaw moment $M_z$ via the $76.2\,\mathrm{mm}$ inter-hand lever arm; $(\Delta v_{z,L}, \Delta v_{z,R})$ modulate tangential quasi-stick shear along $z_{\mathrm{grip}}$ to satisfy $F_{z,L} + F_{z,R} = F_{\mathrm{req},z}$ and the pitch moment $M_y$; $\Delta v_x$ regulates shaft-axis force $F_x$; and $\Delta \omega_x$ regulates shaft-axis roll torque $M_x$:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Grasp-synthesis closure (solver residual, not acceptance evidence):} Because \texttt{solve\_force\_regulated\_hand\_frames} solves $\mathbf{G}\mathbf{y}(\mathbf{q}) = \mathbf{w}_{\mathrm{req,mid}}$ using the shared \texttt{topology\_side\_wrench} law against the analytic \texttt{ClubInertialProperties} target, the $1.85 \times 10^{-13}$--$2.88 \times 10^{-12}$ peak / $9.22 \times 10^{-13}$--$1.93 \times 10^{-12}$ RMS force and $9.93 \times 10^{-15}$--$3.34 \times 10^{-13}$ peak / $3.71 \times 10^{-12}$--$6.84 \times 10^{-12}$ RMS moment differences against \texttt{club\_inverse\_dynamics\_requirement} (\texttt{solver\_closure\_*}, tested in \texttt{test\_grasp\_synthesis\_solver\_closure\_residual}) verify root-solver convergence and adapter site-mapping consistency, not physical acceptance.
  \item \textbf{Acceptance Gate 1 --- Independent MuJoCo \texttt{mj\_inverse} Requirement:} Against MuJoCo \texttt{mj\_inverse} with analytic \texttt{qacc}, relative force errors drop from $596.59 / 509.84$ (\texttt{driver overlap/interlock}), $716.11 / 611.94$ (\texttt{driver ten\_finger}), $449.56 / 384.21$ (\texttt{iron overlap/interlock}), and $539.67 / 461.19$ (\texttt{iron ten\_finger}) to $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{peak}} \le 2.88 \times 10^{-12} \ll 0.05$ and $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{rms}} \le 1.93 \times 10^{-12} \ll 0.02$, and relative COM moment errors drop from $1328.52$--$2182.02$ to $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{peak}} \le 3.37 \times 10^{-13} \ll 0.05$ and $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{rms}} \le 6.84 \times 10^{-12} \ll 0.02$. Against MuJoCo \texttt{mj\_inverse} with second-order central-difference \texttt{qacc} of the prescribed \texttt{qvel}, force peak/RMS errors are $0.433\% / 0.713\%$ (driver) and $0.358\% / 0.635\%$ (7-iron), and COM moment peak/RMS errors are $3.080\% / 1.408\%$ (both within the $5\%$ peak / $2\%$ RMS M3 tolerances, governed by the $\mathcal{O}((\Omega\Delta t)^2) \approx 1.64\%$ interior / $3.08\%$ boundary finite-difference truncation error on the $21$-step sinusoid; Table~\ref{tab:hrg-gs-m8-parity}).
  \item \textbf{Acceptance Gate 2 --- Open-Loop Forward Replay (\texttt{mj\_step}):} In commit \texttt{96405cce03}, the prescribed trajectory set $\mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{origin}}(t) = [0.002\sin(\Omega t), 0, 0]^{\top}\,\mathrm{m}$ and $\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{club}}(t) = \mathbf{I}_3$ while prescribing $\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{origin}}(t) = [0.02\cos(\Omega t), 0, 0]^{\top}\,\mathrm{m/s}$ and $\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathrm{club}}(t) = [0, 0, 0.15\sin(\Omega t)]^{\top}\,\mathrm{rad/s}$ ($\dot{\mathbf{p}}_{\mathrm{origin}} \neq \mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{origin}}$, $\dot{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathrm{club}} \neq [\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathrm{club}}]_{\times}\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{club}}$), causing open-loop forward replay to drift by $2.2903\,\mathrm{mm}$ COM position error ($114.5\%$ of amplitude) and $2.3969\,\mathrm{mrad}$ orientation error. With the kinematically consistent state $\mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{origin}}(t) = \int_0^t \mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{origin}}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau$ and $\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{club}}(t) = \mathbf{R}_z(\int_0^t \omega_z(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau)$, open-loop forward replay (\texttt{replay\_club\_forward\_trajectory}, stepping the free \texttt{golf\_club} from $t = 0$ over $T = 0.04\,\mathrm{s}$ via \texttt{mj\_step} with MuJoCo's first-order \texttt{semi\_implicit\_euler} integrator at $N_{\mathrm{sub}} = 10$ substeps of $\Delta t_{\mathrm{sub}} = 0.2\,\mathrm{ms}$, linearly interpolated hand contact wrenches on \texttt{xfrc\_applied}, zero active club welds, and gravity active) tracks the prescribed trajectory with COM position peak/RMS error $0.0065 / 0.0045\,\mathrm{mm}$ ($1.47\% / 1.02\%$ of the $0.4417\,\mathrm{mm}$ COM motion amplitude) on driver and $0.0058 / 0.0041\,\mathrm{mm}$ ($1.52\% / 1.09\%$ of the $0.3784\,\mathrm{mm}$ COM motion amplitude) on 7-iron, and orientation peak/RMS error $0.0246 / 0.0139\,\mathrm{mrad}$ ($1.29\% / 0.73\%$ of the $1.9099\,\mathrm{mrad}$ rotation amplitude) on both clubs (Table~\ref{tab:hrg-gs-m8-parity}).
  \item \textbf{Explicit \S 19 squeeze, nullspace split, and quasi-stick friction-cone ratios:} At $t = T$, the normal squeeze forces are $(F_{\mathrm{squeeze},L}, F_{\mathrm{squeeze},R}) = (206.83, 207.38)\,\mathrm{N}$ (\texttt{driver overlap/interlock}), $(210.13, 237.07)\,\mathrm{N}$ (\texttt{driver ten\_finger}), $(207.27, 208.04)\,\mathrm{N}$ (\texttt{iron overlap/interlock}), and $(210.63, 238.27)\,\mathrm{N}$ (\texttt{iron ten\_finger}), exceeding $F_{\mathrm{squeeze,min}}(T) = \|\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{req}}(T)\|_2 / \mu_s$ ($3.830\,\mathrm{N}$ driver, $5.064\,\mathrm{N}$ 7-iron at $\mu_s = 0.90$) by $> 50\times$. The terminal nullspace split $\mathbf{s}(T) = \frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{w}_R - \mathbf{w}_L)$ is $(8.825, 0.000, 10.639, 0.223, 0.016, 0.018)$ (\texttt{driver overlap/interlock}), $(7.238, 0.000, 27.130, -0.471, 0.013, -0.050)$ (\texttt{driver ten\_finger}), $(7.986, 0.000, 13.288, 0.230, 0.022, 0.017)$ (\texttt{iron overlap/interlock}), and $(6.422, 0.000, 30.087, -0.477, 0.018, -0.050)$ (\texttt{iron ten\_finger}). Every hand--club pad remains in compression and regularized quasi-stick with $\|\mathbf{v}_{t,i}(T)\| \in [0.049, 0.091]\,\mathrm{mm/s} < v_{\mathrm{tr}} = 1.0\,\mathrm{mm/s}$ and $\|\mathbf{f}_{t,i}(T)\| / (\mu_s f_{n,i}(T)) \in [0.0490, 0.0904] \ll 1.0$.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Limitations.}
Muscle recruitment across the $78$ \texttt{myoHand} musculotendon actuators ($39$ per hand) is explicitly out of scope for \texttt{HRG-GS-M8}: all actuators remain inactive (\texttt{ctrl = 0}, \texttt{act = 0}) and finger joints are locked via equality constraints in the frozen-finger qualification mode. Furthermore, this bilateral scene is an isolated two-hand + club grip fixture driven via wrist mocap welds rather than a full-body swing simulation, tangential force regulation relies on regularized Stribeck quasi-stick ($\|\mathbf{v}_t\| \le 0.091\,\mathrm{mm/s} < v_{\mathrm{tr}} = 1.0\,\mathrm{mm/s}$) rather than a stateful bristle/elastoplastic stiction model at exact zero slip, and the hyperstatic per-hand wrench split $\mathbf{s} \in \ker(\mathbf{G})$ and internal preload squeeze ($\delta_0 = 0.25\,\mathrm{mm}$) remain mechanically indeterminate from club inverse dynamics alone and are selected by the explicit bimanual lever-arm parameterization $\mathbf{q}(t)$. In addition, for \texttt{iron7}, MuJoCo's \texttt{boundinertia=".0001"} compiler floor clamps the axial shaft moment of inertia from $6.492 \times 10^{-5}\,\mathrm{kg\cdot m^2}$ up to $1.000 \times 10^{-4}\,\mathrm{kg\cdot m^2}$.

\textbf{Reproduction Commands.}
\begin{verbatim}
bash scripts/setup_myosuite_models.sh
python3 -m pytest tests/unit/grip_contact/test_myohand_bilateral_grip.py
python3 -m pytest tests/unit/grip_contact -m "not slow"
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{OpenSim Topology Grip Friction Mapping and Swing Parity (HRG-GS-G7b)}
\label{sec:hrg-gs-g7b}
\textbf{Diagnosis of Eight Root Causes.} Investigation of the HRG-GS-G10 OpenSim swing net-wrench divergence ($319.75\%$ peak / $91.60\%$ RMS on \texttt{overlap}/\texttt{interlock} swing and $5995.09\%$ peak / $1334.63\%$ RMS on \texttt{ten\_finger} swing over $0.04$\,s, alongside $35.8\%$ tangential-force divergence and $40\text{--}50\%$ contact-point drift) and the subsequent \texttt{ten\_finger} capsule parity audit via single-pair sliding-friction probes, trajectory boundary inspection, and identical-state per-pair instrumentation of \texttt{ten\_finger\_little\_finger\_grip} (\texttt{trail\_little\_finger}) isolated eight distinct analytical and numerical root causes:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,noitemsep]
\item \textit{Prescribed-Motion Spline Horizon Truncation (\path{grip_contact_osim_sim.py}):} \texttt{simulate\_grip\_contact} sliced \texttt{swing.time\_s} and \texttt{swing.q} at \texttt{t\_end\_s} ($0.040$\,s) before constructing \texttt{ContactGripSimulator}. When OpenSim's implicit BDF integrator (\texttt{CPodes}) evaluated trial steps slightly beyond $t_{\mathrm{last}} = 0.040$\,s to interpolate the final sample state, \texttt{SimmSpline::calcDerivative} switched from cubic spline interpolation to linear extrapolation ($\ddot{q}(t) \equiv 0$ for $t > t_{\mathrm{last}}$). This injected a $\sim 90$\,rad/s$^2$ prescribed-hand acceleration step discontinuity at $t = 0.040$\,s, producing the $319.75\%$ (\texttt{overlap}/\texttt{interlock}) and $5995.09\%$ (\texttt{ten\_finger}) boundary force spikes at the final sample. Passing the full trajectory horizon (\texttt{full\_times}) to \texttt{SimmSpline} while integrating to \texttt{t\_end=float(times[-1])} removes the boundary discontinuity completely.
\item \textit{BDF Integrator Tolerance (\texttt{TOPOLOGY\_CONTACT\_ACCURACY = 5e-8}):} \texttt{grip\_contact\_osim\_sim.py} originally ran \texttt{CPodes} at \texttt{CONTACT\_ACCURACY = 1e-4}, whereas Pinocchio, Drake, and the single-pad feasibility probe in \path{test_opensim_contact_grip.py} use $10^{-6}$. At $10^{-4}$ tolerance on the stiff $171$\,kN/m contact foundation, a $3\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ local truncation error produces $5\text{--}8$\,N transient force spikes across opposing pads. Furthermore, in \texttt{ten\_finger}, \texttt{ten\_finger\_little\_finger\_grip} radially opposes \texttt{lead\_heel\_pad\_butt} at $t = 0$ ($\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{net,ref}}(0) = \mathbf{0}$), making the determinate denominators $10.4\text{--}15.0\times$ smaller than in \texttt{overlap}/\texttt{interlock} ($\max_t\|\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{net,ref}}\| = 3.4890$\,N hold / $4.0995$\,N swing vs.\ $42.7738$\,N; $\max_t\|\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{com,ref}}\| = 2.5340\,\mathrm{N\cdot m}$ vs.\ $37.9864\,\mathrm{N\cdot m}$), so $2.0\%$ RMS equals only $0.0698\text{--}0.0820$\,N and $0.0507\,\mathrm{N\cdot m}$. At $10^{-6}$ BDF accuracy, \texttt{CPodes} step-size adaptations during prescribed swing acceleration still produced $0.5534$\,N force spikes at $t = 0.010$\,s, inflating \texttt{ten\_finger} swing RMS from $2.605\%$ to $5.070\%$ (\texttt{net\_force}) and from $0.930\%$ to $2.918\%$ (\texttt{net\_moment\_com}) over $0.04$\,s (and $4.856\% / 4.437\%$ over $0.10$\,s). Setting \texttt{TOPOLOGY\_CONTACT\_ACCURACY = 5e-8} for topology runs (while retaining \texttt{CONTACT\_ACCURACY = 1e-4} for the legacy \texttt{PadContactModel} path) eliminates BDF step chattering across both hold and swing.
\item \textit{IEEE-754 ULP Ring-Count Rounding in \texttt{capped\_cylinder\_mesh} (\path{grip_mesh.py}):} For the lead hand (\texttt{L}) grip cylinder axial span $[-0.036, 0.120]$\,m at $1.0$\,mm ring pitch, floating-point division yielded $(\mathrm{hi} - \mathrm{lo}) / \Delta z = 156.00000000000003$, which \texttt{math.ceil} rounded up to $157$ rings ($0.99363$\,mm pitch), whereas the trail hand (\texttt{R}) span $[-0.180, -0.024]$\,m yielded $156.0$ ($156$ rings, $1.00000$\,mm pitch). This shifted lead-hand pads off the mesh vertex rings onto triangle faces with a $0.33$\,N static $z$-force bias and $6.6\%$ tangential asymmetry. Subtracting a $10^{-12}$ ULP guard before \texttt{math.ceil} restores exact $1.00000$\,mm ring pitch on both hands.
\item \textit{Per-Capsule Ring-Aligned \texttt{ContactMesh} Eliminating Static Boundary-Triangle Bias (\path{full_body_grip_contact.py}):} All $9$ spherical pad sites sit at integer-millimeter axial coordinates ($\pm 15, \pm 12, -20$\,mm) coinciding with vertex rings of \texttt{grip\_mesh\_\{side\}.obj} and evaluate with $f_t(0) = 0.000000$\,N. By contrast, \texttt{trail\_little\_finger} is placed by inter-finger abutment at $x_{\mathrm{hand}} = -36.958199$\,mm ($p_0 = -44.9582$\,mm, $p_1 = -28.9582$\,mm), which is $+0.041801$\,mm off the $1.0$\,mm vertex rings. For $r_s = 7.5$\,mm and $\delta_0 = 0.50$\,mm, the axial contact patch half-width $a_x = \sqrt{2 r_s \delta_0 - \delta_0^2} = 2.69258$\,mm clears the triangle centroid rings at $\pm 2\tfrac{2}{3} = \pm 2.66667$\,mm by only $0.02591$\,mm. Shifting the sphere centers by $+0.041801\,\mathrm{mm} > 0.02591\,\mathrm{mm}$ drops the $-2.6667$\,mm centroid ring outside the contact patch ($2.7085 > 2.6926$\,mm) while keeping the $+2.6667$\,mm ring inside ($2.6249 < 2.6926$\,mm). That single unbalanced chiral triangle ring generated a spurious static force $\mathbf{f}_{\mathrm{hand},k}(0) = [-5.172\times 10^{-3}, -8.559721, +1.400\times 10^{-3}]^{\top}$\,N per sphere, summing to $\Delta\mathbf{f}_{\mathrm{hand}}(0) = [-0.02586, -0.02485, +0.00700]^{\top}$\,N ($f_n(0) = 42.798604$\,N vs.\ $42.773752$\,N, $f_t(0) = 0.026793$\,N, $\|\Delta\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{net}}(0)\| = 0.03655$\,N) and $\|\Delta\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{com}}(0)\| = 0.02111\,\mathrm{N\cdot m}$---accounting for $100\%$ of the $0.833\%$ \texttt{net\_moment\_com} peak error across all \texttt{ten\_finger} runs. Emitting a dedicated closed-cylinder \texttt{ContactMesh} (\texttt{grip\_mesh\_\{pair.name\}.obj}) centered on each capsule site's axial midpoint $x_{\mathrm{mid}} = x_{\mathrm{centre}} + c_{x,0}$ with half-span $\lceil(L + r_s + m_{\mathrm{margin}})/\Delta z - 10^{-12}\rceil \Delta z$ aligns all $5$ capsule spheres ($\{-8, -4, 0, +4, +8\}$\,mm relative to $x_{\mathrm{mid}}$) with exact vertex rings, reducing $\|\Delta\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{net}}(0)\|$ by $4{,}100\times$ to $8.90 \times 10^{-6}$\,N ($f_t(0) = 2.68 \times 10^{-7}$\,N) and \texttt{net\_moment\_com} peak error from $0.833\%$ to $\mathbf{0.000\%}$.
\item \textit{Normal-Force-Weighted Center of Pressure in \texttt{\_sample\_topology\_side} (\path{grip_contact_osim_sim.py}):} \texttt{\_sample\_topology\_side} previously took \texttt{contact\_point\_m} and \texttt{normal} from the final loop index $k = N - 1$ (distal capsule endpoint $p_1$ at $x_{\mathrm{hand}} = -28.958$\,mm, $8.0$\,mm away from the midpoint at $t = 0$). Computing the normal-force-weighted center of pressure across the sphere chain,
\begin{equation}
\bar{\mathbf{r}}_{\mathrm{rel}} = \frac{\sum_{k=0}^{N-1} f_{n,k}\,\mathbf{r}_{\mathrm{rel},k}}{\sum_{k=0}^{N-1} f_{n,k}}, \qquad
\bar{u}_{\mathrm{rad}} = \frac{\sum_{k=0}^{N-1} f_{n,k}\,\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\mathrm{rad},k}}{\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{N-1} f_{n,k}\,\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\mathrm{rad},k}\right\|_2},
\end{equation}
places the reported contact point at the true effective line of action ($x_{\mathrm{hand}} = -36.958$\,mm at $t = 0$, matching Pinocchio's $s = 0.5000$ to $< 10^{-6}$\,m) and reduces \texttt{ten\_finger} \texttt{contact\_point\_drift} peak/RMS from $8.95\% / 6.72\%$ to $4.293\% / 2.616\%$ ($0.04$\,s hold) and from $8.51\% / 5.06\%$ to $1.212\% / 3.498\%$ ($0.04$\,s swing).
\item \textit{Quadratic Winkler Operating Point ($\delta_0 = 2\delta_{\mathrm{preload}}$) and Normal Tangent Stiffness:} The shared Hunt--Crossley normal law $F_{\mathrm{HC}}(\delta) = k_{\mathrm{law}}\delta$ has constant tangent stiffness $F'_{\mathrm{HC}}(\delta_{\mathrm{preload}}) = k_{\mathrm{law}} = 171\,095$\,N/m at $\delta_{\mathrm{preload}} = 0.25$\,mm ($f_{\mathrm{target}} = 42.7738$\,N). By contrast, OpenSim's Winkler foundation force is quadratic in penetration depth:
\begin{equation}
F_{\mathrm{Winkler}}(\delta) = k_W \Phi \delta^2 \implies \frac{\mathrm{d}F_{\mathrm{Winkler}}}{\mathrm{d}\delta}(\delta_0) = \frac{2 F_{\mathrm{Winkler}}(\delta_0)}{\delta_0}.
\end{equation}
Calibrating \texttt{calibrated\_site\_foundation} at $\delta_0 = \delta_{\mathrm{preload}} = 0.25$\,mm yielded $\mathrm{d}F_{\mathrm{Winkler}}/\mathrm{d}\delta = 2 f_{\mathrm{target}}/\delta_{\mathrm{preload}} = 2 k_{\mathrm{law}} = 342\,190$\,N/m (measured $359\,533.8$\,N/m on the discrete mesh), making the OpenSim grip $2\times$ too stiff in translation and tilt and causing the $40\text{--}50\%$ deficit in \texttt{contact\_point\_drift} and \texttt{deflection\_L/R}. Matching both the preload force $F_{\mathrm{Winkler}}(\delta_0) = f_{\mathrm{target}}$ and the tangent stiffness $F'_{\mathrm{Winkler}}(\delta_0) = k_{\mathrm{law}}/\mathrm{count}$ uniquely requires:
\begin{equation}
\delta_0 = 2\delta_{\mathrm{preload}} = 0.50\,\mathrm{mm}, \qquad
k_W = \frac{f_{\mathrm{target}}}{\Phi \delta_0^2},
\end{equation}
consistent with \texttt{elastic\_foundation\_for\_law} in \path{elastic_foundation.py}. Each topology \texttt{ContactSphere} center is shifted radially inward by $\delta_0 - \delta_{\mathrm{preload}} = 0.25$\,mm via \texttt{shift\_topology\_site\_inward} so its neutral pose penetration equals $\delta_0$, while \texttt{\_index\_topology\_records} retains the unshifted anatomical center for physical penetration and contact-point drift reporting. Furthermore, for opposing pads at $\pm\delta_{\mathrm{disp}}$, the second-order Winkler convexity $+\tfrac{1}{4}f_{\mathrm{target}}(\delta_{\mathrm{disp}}/\delta_{\mathrm{preload}})^2$ cancels identically in the 3D vector sum ($\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{net}}$, $\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{com}}$) but adds with the same positive sign in unsigned scalar normal-force sums (\texttt{squeeze\_n}); projecting each sphere's unsigned scalar normal force onto the Hunt--Crossley tangent line via the exact analytical ratio
\begin{equation}
\frac{F_{\mathrm{HC}}(\delta_{\mathrm{phys},k}, \dot{\delta}_k)}{F_{\mathrm{Winkler}}(\delta_k, \dot{\delta}_k)} = \frac{2(\delta_k/\delta_0) - 1}{(\delta_k/\delta_0)^2} = 1 - \left(\frac{\delta_k - \delta_0}{\delta_k}\right)^2, \qquad \delta_k = \delta_{\mathrm{phys},k} + (\delta_0 - \delta_{\mathrm{preload}}),
\end{equation}
preserves exact linear-law scalar squeeze parity ($\le 1.03\%$ peak, $\le 0.94\%$ RMS across all styles and motions).
\item \textit{Stribeck vs.\ Simbody Hollars Tangential Friction Mapping:} The shared tangential friction coefficient in \path{pad_contact.py} is
\begin{equation}
\mu_{\mathrm{law}}(v) = \left[\mu_d + (\mu_s - \mu_d)\exp\!\left(-\left(\frac{v}{v_{\mathrm{tr}}}\right)^2\right) + \mu_v v\right]\tanh\!\left(\frac{v}{v_{\mathrm{tr}}}\right),
\end{equation}
whereas OpenSim's \texttt{ElasticFoundationForce} evaluates Simbody's Hollars smoothing (with $s(x) = x^2(3 - 2x)$ for $x \in [0, 1]$):
\begin{equation}
\mu_{\mathrm{Simbody}}(v) = \begin{cases}
s(v/v_{\mathrm{tr}})\,\tilde{\mu}_s, & v < v_{\mathrm{tr}}, \\
\tilde{\mu}_s - s\!\left(\min\!\left(\tfrac{v - v_{\mathrm{tr}}}{2v_{\mathrm{tr}}}, 1\right)\right)(\tilde{\mu}_s - \tilde{\mu}_d) + \tilde{\mu}_v v, & v \ge v_{\mathrm{tr}}.
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
For $\mu_s = 0.9$, $\mu_d = 0.7$, $\mu_v = 0.1$\,s/m, and $v_{\mathrm{tr}} = 10^{-3}$\,m/s, $\mu_{\mathrm{law}}(v)$ is monotonically increasing with no static peak above $\mu_d = 0.7$ ($\mu_{\mathrm{law}}(v_{\mathrm{tr}}) = [0.7 + 0.2 e^{-1}]\tanh(1) = 0.58915$), whereas passing $\tilde{\mu}_s = \mu_s = 0.9$ to Simbody creates a peak of $\mu_{\mathrm{Simbody}}(v_{\mathrm{tr}}) = 0.9001$ ($+52.8\%$ overshoot at $v = v_{\mathrm{tr}}$ and $28.45\%$ peak error across the sliding sweep). Because $\max_{v \le 3v_{\mathrm{tr}}}(\mu_{\mathrm{law}}(v) - \mu_v v) = \mu_d$, mapping $\tilde{\mu}_s = \tilde{\mu}_d = \mu_d = 0.7$ eliminates the spurious Stribeck peak and aligns $\mu_{\mathrm{Simbody}}(v)$ with $\mu_{\mathrm{law}}(v)$ across the single-pair sliding sweep ($v_s \in [10^{-4}, 1.0]$\,m/s) to \textbf{$1.832\%$ peak / $4.768\%$ RMS} (within the $5\%$ tolerance).
\item \textit{Identical-State Per-Pair Instrumentation of \texttt{ten\_finger\_little\_finger\_grip} (Confirming the $8.0$\,mm Capsule Moment-Arm Shift While Refuting It as the Driver of the $5.070\%$ Swing Failure):} Evaluating OpenSim's 5-sphere chain ($x_{\mathrm{hand}} \in [-44.958, -28.958]$\,mm) and Pinocchio's \texttt{capsule\_capsule} (\texttt{closest\_points\_segments}) at identical OpenSim states confirms a geometric closest-point jump in the single-point reference: at $t = 0.000$\,s ($\theta = 0$, $\sin^2\theta < 10^{-10}$), \texttt{closest\_points\_segments} selects the capsule midpoint $s = 0.5000$ ($x_{\mathrm{hand}} = -36.958$\,mm, world moment arm $\mathbf{r}_{\mathrm{arm}} = \mathbf{c} - \mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{com}} = [0.2647, 0.2896, 0.4494]^{\top}$\,m), matching OpenSim's CoP ($x_{\mathrm{eff}} = -36.958$\,mm) with $f_{n,\mathrm{OSim}} = f_{n,\mathrm{Pin}} = 42.7738$\,N and $\|\Delta\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{com}}(0)\| = 0.0000\,\mathrm{N\cdot m}$. For $t \ge 0.002$\,s (hold) or $t \ge 0.004$\,s (swing), once club pitch tilt exceeds $\theta = 10^{-5}$\,rad ($\sin^2\theta \ge 10^{-10}$), Pinocchio's single-point closest-point parameter jumps discontinuously from $s = 0.5000$ to the proximal endpoint $s = 0.0000$ ($p_0$ at $x_{\mathrm{hand}} = -44.958$\,mm, world $\mathbf{c}_{\mathrm{Pin}} = [-0.0466, -0.0299, 0.8090]^{\top}$\,m, $\mathbf{r}_{\mathrm{arm,Pin}} = [0.2604, 0.2962, 0.4475]^{\top}$\,m at $t = 0.004$\,s), whereas OpenSim's 5 spheres remain in simultaneous contact ($f_{n,k} = [10.02, 9.27, 8.53, 7.80, 7.09]$\,N at $t = 0.004$\,s; $[12.83, 10.61, 8.50, 6.50, 4.62]$\,N at $t = 0.040$\,s), shifting the CoP smoothly to $x_{\mathrm{eff}} = -38.22$\,mm ($t = 0.004$\,s) and $-40.66$\,mm ($t = 0.040$\,s; $\mathbf{r}_{\mathrm{arm,OSim}} = [0.2647, 0.2896, 0.4494]^{\top}$\,m). At identical states, normal and tangential forces on \texttt{ten\_finger\_little\_finger\_grip} agree to within $|f_{n,\mathrm{OSim}} - f_{n,\mathrm{Pin}}| \le 1.38$\,N ($42.705$\,N vs.\ $42.548$\,N at $t = 0.004$\,s; $43.063$\,N vs.\ $41.685$\,N at $t = 0.040$\,s) and $|f_{t,\mathrm{OSim}} - f_{t,\mathrm{Pin}}| \le 0.044$\,N ($0.072$\,N vs.\ $0.088$\,N at $t = 0.004$\,s; $0.273$\,N vs.\ $0.258$\,N at $t = 0.040$\,s), while the $6.3\text{--}8.0$\,mm axial CoP offset creates an identical-state per-pair moment difference $\|\Delta\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{com}}\| = 0.364\text{--}0.448\,\mathrm{N\cdot m}$ and a step-1 couple impulse at $t = 0.002$\,s ($\|\Delta\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{net}}(t_1)\| = 0.2022$\,N vs.\ $0.0056$\,N for a single sphere at $p_0$). However, in dynamic equilibrium the 10-pad grip adjusts club pitch by $\Delta\theta \approx 0.015$\,mrad so that total $\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{com}}$ matches Pinocchio to $\mathbf{0.000\%}$ peak / $\mathbf{0.705\%}$ RMS ($0.04$\,s hold) and $\mathbf{0.930\%}$ RMS ($0.04$\,s swing), refuting the capsule moment arm as the driver of the prior $5.070\% / 2.918\%$ swing failure.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Before/After Parity Summary ($0.04$\,s and $0.10$\,s Hold and Swing).} Table~\ref{tab:g7b_before_after_parity} compares the OpenSim topology grip errors against the Pinocchio reference before and after the HRG-GS-G7b analytical mapping, spline-horizon, ring-aligned capsule \texttt{ContactMesh}, CoP reporting, and $5\times 10^{-8}$ BDF accuracy fixes. Over $0.10$\,s, $100\%$ of styles (\texttt{overlap}, \texttt{interlock}, \texttt{ten\_finger}) and motions (\texttt{hold}, \texttt{swing}) now pass both determinate metrics (\texttt{net\_force} and \texttt{net\_moment\_com}); over $0.04$\,s, all \texttt{net\_moment\_com} checks pass and only \texttt{ten\_finger} \texttt{net\_force} RMS ($2.588\%$ hold, $2.605\%$ swing) exceeds $2.0\%$.

\textbf{Failed Experiments and Residual $0.04$\,s \texttt{ten\_finger} \texttt{net\_force} Limitation.}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,noitemsep]
\item \textit{Shifting \texttt{\_index\_topology\_records} sample coordinates inward by $\delta_0 - \delta_{\mathrm{preload}}$:} Evaluated and rejected because \texttt{\_sample\_topology\_side} uses the indexed coordinates to report physical penetration $\delta_{\mathrm{phys}}$ and radial unit normals; shifting the reported centers biased the neutral penetration report from $0.25$\,mm to $0.50$\,mm. Keeping \texttt{\_index\_topology\_records} at the unshifted anatomical centers while shifting only the OpenSim \texttt{ContactSphere} geometry locations preserves exact geometric reporting.
\item \textit{Unprojected Winkler scalar normal-force sum at $\delta_0 = 2\delta_{\mathrm{preload}}$:} Evaluated and rejected because the second-order Winkler convexity $+\tfrac{1}{4}f_{\mathrm{target}}(\Delta\delta/\delta_{\mathrm{preload}})^2$ adds constructively across opposing $\pm y$ pads in the unsigned scalar sum \texttt{squeeze\_n}, raising \texttt{ten\_finger} lead-hand squeeze RMS to $2.05\%$ (above the $2.0\%$ G7 limit). Projecting each sphere's unsigned scalar normal force onto the Hunt--Crossley tangent line via $1 - ((\delta_k - \delta_0)/\delta_k)^2$ reduces lead/trail squeeze RMS to $0.30\text{--}0.94\%$ across all styles.
\item \textit{Concentrating \texttt{trail\_little\_finger} into a single sphere at $p_0$ ($s = 0$) or midpoint ($s = 0.5$):} Evaluated and rejected. Placing a single sphere at $p_0$ ($x_{\mathrm{hand}} = -44.958$\,mm) matches Pinocchio's tilted closest point ($s = 0$) for $t \ge 0.002$\,s and reduces the step-1 force difference $\|\Delta\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{net}}(t_1)\|$ from $0.2022$\,N to $0.0056$\,N, but introduces a $0.3636\,\mathrm{N\cdot m}$ ($14.345\%$ peak) moment error at $t = 0$ because Pinocchio's \texttt{closest\_points\_segments} evaluates parallel segments ($\theta = 0$) at the midpoint $s = 0.5000$. Conversely, placing a single sphere at the midpoint $s = 0.5$ leaves the $0.04$\,s \texttt{net\_force} RMS at $2.589\%$ (hold) / $2.596\%$ (swing) while losing capsule rotational roll resistance.
\item \textit{Residual \texttt{ten\_finger} RMS Error ($2.60\%$ on $0.04$\,s hold \texttt{net\_force}; $5.07\% / 2.92\%$ on $0.04$\,s swing; against the \#13308 line-contact reference $4.577\%$ hold and $2.374\%$ swing, and $2.970\%$ on the $0.10$\,s hold, Section~\ref{sec:hrg-gs-line-contact}):} In \texttt{ten\_finger}, the lead index distal/middle phalanges ($x = -0.025$\,m) and trail little-finger capsules ($x = -0.048$\,m) sit on only one axial side of the grip CoM without an opposing capsule on the same hand, creating a net static pitch couple balanced by club pitch tilt ($\sim 0.4^\circ$). Because OpenSim's \texttt{ElasticFoundationForce} evaluates tangential friction at triangle centroids on the outer cylinder surface ($r = r_{\mathrm{grip}} = 13.5$\,mm) with zero normal-patch torsional resistance ($\tau_n = 0$), whereas Pinocchio's shared law (\path{pad_contact.py}) applies tangential force at the midpoint of the penetration depth ($r_{\mathrm{mid}} = r_{\mathrm{grip}} - \tfrac{1}{2}\delta$) and includes analytical patch spin torque $\tau_n = \tfrac{2}{3}\mu(v_{\mathrm{eff}}) r_{\mathrm{patch}} f_n \tanh(\omega_n / \omega_{\mathrm{tr}})$, the lever-arm and spin-torque difference excites a low-amplitude pitch mode on the asymmetric \texttt{ten\_finger} layout. Over $0.10$\,s static hold, this transient damps out and \texttt{ten\_finger} passes both determinate metrics ($2.412\% / 1.731\%$ on \texttt{net\_force} and $0.833\% / 0.523\%$ on \texttt{net\_moment\_com}); on the short $0.04$\,s window, \texttt{opensim-ten\_finger-hold} and \texttt{opensim-ten\_finger-swing} remain strict \texttt{xfail}s with updated measured numbers and no loosened tolerances.
\end{itemize}

python3 -m pytest tests/unit/grip_contact/test_opensim_topology_grip.py -m "not slow"
python3 -m pytest tests/unit/grip_contact/test_opensim_topology_grip.py
python3 -m pytest tests/unit/grip_contact/test_grip_style_parity_report.py
python3 scripts/grip/grip_style_parity_report.py --duration-s 0.04 --out /tmp/g7b_004

\end{verbatim}

\subsection{OpenSim MSK Grip Adapter (HRG-GS-M4, \#13326)}
\textbf{Method and Architecture.}
The native OpenSim musculoskeletal grip adapter (\texttt{OpenSimMskGripAdapter} in
\path{src/engines/physics_engines/opensim/python/msk_grip_adapter.py}; the club XML stays in \path{msk_club.py}) binds the shared anatomical contact
topology $\mathcal{T}$ (\texttt{ContactTopology}, HRG-GS-G1, \#13299) and rigid-proxy binding layer
(\texttt{grip\_contact.binding}, HRG-GS-M3, \#13325) directly to OpenSim golfer models
(\texttt{golf\_humanoid.osim}, \texttt{golf\_humanoid\_scaled.osim}, and \texttt{bruno2015\_fullbody\_os4\_v2.osim}).
Full-body models with bilateral hand bodies (\texttt{hand\_l}, \texttt{hand\_r}) are admitted through
versioned manifests under \path{src/config/grip_bindings/}; lumbar-only spine models
(\texttt{senteler2016\_lumbspine\_*}) fail closed with \texttt{SchemaFeatureUnavailableError}.
For \texttt{bruno2015\_fullbody\_os4\_v2.osim}, the admission record explicitly documents the native
locked distal forearm and wrist coordinates (\texttt{pro\_sup\_l/r}, \texttt{wrist\_flex\_l/r},
\texttt{wrist\_dev\_l/r}).

The adapter supports both club attachment modes of \texttt{attach\_club\_to\_model} across all three
grip styles (\texttt{overlap}, \texttt{interlock}, \texttt{ten\_finger}) and optional trigger-finger
variation:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Weld + Constraint Mode (\texttt{attachment="weld"})}: The club is welded to \texttt{hand\_l}
via \texttt{WeldJoint} and closed to \texttt{hand\_r} via \texttt{WeldConstraint}. Because the hands and
club form a kinematically welded loop, \texttt{GripCapabilities} declares
\texttt{supports\_physical\_pairs = False} with a non-empty \texttt{unavailable\_reason}. Contact evaluation
operates in allocated/reporting mode only; calling \texttt{apply\_wrenches} or \texttt{apply\_report}
raises \texttt{ValueError} so external point wrenches are never silently applied to welded bodies and never
cancel inside a constraint loop.
\item \textbf{Free Club + Two Bushings Mode (\texttt{attachment="bushing"})}: The club possesses a
6-DOF \texttt{FreeJoint} to ground and interacts with \texttt{hand\_l} and \texttt{hand\_r} through
spatial wrenches applied via OpenSim \texttt{PrescribedForce} elements (\texttt{Club\_grip\_contact\_pf},
\texttt{hand\_l\_grip\_contact\_pf}, \texttt{hand\_r\_grip\_contact\_pf}). When shared-law wrenches are
applied, the native \texttt{lead\_grip\_bushing} and \texttt{trail\_grip\_bushing} elements are disabled
in the state (\texttt{setAppliesForce(state, False)}) so native and shared-law contact are never applied
twice.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Equations and Parameters.}
At each kinematic state $s$, the adapter reads world poses and spatial velocities of \texttt{hand\_l},
\texttt{hand\_r}, and \texttt{Club} from OpenSim's Simbody matter subsystem. To satisfy the neutral
preload penetration $\delta_0 = 0.25$\,mm ($|\delta - \delta_0| \le 0.5$\,mm) across all physical
pad--grip sites at the calibrated address pose ($q = 0$) and prevent rigid-proxy pad opposition from
causing net radial drift on the 6-DOF free club, the adapter decomposes the relative hand--club offset
into the calibrated rigid-proxy topology frames ($\bar{T}_{\mathrm{L}}, \bar{T}_{\mathrm{R}}, \bar{T}_{\mathrm{C}}$)
plus the instantaneous relative displacement $(\Delta p_h, R_{\mathrm{rel},h})$ of each hand $h \in \{\mathrm{L}, \mathrm{R}\}$
with respect to its grip attachment frame $p_{\mathrm{grip},h}$:
\begin{equation}
\tilde{p}_h = \bar{p}_h + R_{\mathrm{C}}^\top \bigl(p_h(s) - p_{\mathrm{grip},h}(s)\bigr), \qquad
\tilde{R}_h = \bar{R}_h R_{\mathrm{rel},h}(s).
\end{equation}
Each physical pad--grip pair $(i, j) \in \mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{phys}}$ is evaluated through the shared
Kelvin--Voigt normal and regularized Coulomb tangential law (\texttt{evaluate\_contacts}), while its
dynamic restoring contribution is referenced to the neutral preload equilibrium wrench
$\bar{w}_{ij} = w_{ij}(\bar{T}_{\mathrm{L}}, \bar{T}_{\mathrm{R}}, \bar{T}_{\mathrm{C}})$:
\begin{equation}
\Delta w_{ij}(s) = w_{ij}\bigl(\tilde{T}_{\mathrm{L}}(s), \tilde{T}_{\mathrm{R}}(s), \bar{T}_{\mathrm{C}}\bigr) - \bar{w}_{ij}.
\end{equation}
Combined with the per-hand translational and rotational bushing compliance $(K_t, K_r, B_t, B_r)$, the
resulting world-frame forces $f_b$ and moments $\tau_b$ about each body origin $p_b(s)$ verify exact
action--reaction balance:
\begin{equation}
\sum_{b \in \{\mathrm{hand\_l}, \mathrm{hand\_r}, \mathrm{Club}\}} f_b = 0, \qquad
\sum_{b \in \{\mathrm{hand\_l}, \mathrm{hand\_r}, \mathrm{Club}\}} \bigl(\tau_b + p_b(s) \times f_b\bigr) = 0,
\end{equation}
while allocated hand--hand pairs $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{alloc}}$ are solved via \texttt{allocate\_hand\_hand\_pairs}
and excluded from the physical body wrenches (\texttt{applied\_allocated\_force\_n} $= 0$).

\textbf{Measured Parity and Qualification.}
Table~\ref{tab:osim_msk_grip_parity} summarizes the measured forward-dynamics net club wrench parity of
\texttt{simulate\_msk\_grip\_hold} ($0.05$\,s at $\Delta t = 1$\,ms on \texttt{golf\_humanoid.osim} in
bushing mode) against the baseline native \texttt{msk\_club} bushing path, together with the M3
law-level qualification status (\texttt{compare\_topology\_series}):
\begin{table}[htbp]
\centering
\small
\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}
\toprule
\textbf{Style} & \textbf{$\delta_0$ (mm)} & \textbf{Force Peak} & \textbf{Force RMS} & \textbf{Moment Peak} & \textbf{Moment RMS} & \textbf{M3 Status} \\
\midrule
Overlap & $0.250$ & $0.014\%$ & $0.028\%$ & $0.070\%$ & $0.094\%$ & \texttt{passed} \\
Interlock & $0.250$ & $0.014\%$ & $0.028\%$ & $0.070\%$ & $0.094\%$ & \texttt{passed} \\
Ten-Finger & $0.250$ & $0.121\%$ & $0.096\%$ & $0.004\%$ & $0.071\%$ & \texttt{passed} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{OpenSim MSK grip adapter preload penetration, net club wrench parity vs native bushing baseline, and M3 qualification verdict (limits: $|\delta_0| \le 0.5$\,mm, peak $\le 5.0\%$, RMS $\le 2.0\%$).}
\label{tab:osim_msk_grip_parity}
\end{table}

\textbf{Limitations.}
Admitted OpenSim golfer models represent each hand as a single rigid segment (\texttt{hand\_l}, \texttt{hand\_r});
finger phalanges are rigid-proxy sites fixed in the hand frame, and in \texttt{bruno2015\_fullbody\_os4\_v2.osim}
the native forearm pronation/supination and wrist flexion/deviation coordinates are locked by default.
In \texttt{weld} mode, contact forces are diagnostic/allocated only because the hands and club are kinematically welded.

\textbf{Reproduction Command.}
\begin{verbatim}
python3 -m pytest tests/unit/grip_contact/test_opensim_msk_grip_adapter.py \
    tests/opensim/test_msk_club.py
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{Native Pinocchio Golfer Grip Adapter (HRG-GS-M9, \#13331)}
\textbf{Two-Hand Variant Topology and Frame Geometry.}
In the default native Pinocchio golfer (\path{src/engines/physics_engines/pinocchio/models/generated/golfer.urdf}, loaded by \path{src/engines/physics_engines/pinocchio/python/motion_matching/simulate.py} and exported by \path{src/tools/model_converter/urdf_exporter.py}), the club assembly (\texttt{club\_shaft} and \texttt{club\_head}) hangs from a single virtual \texttt{mid\_hands} frame welded to \texttt{thorax3} at translation $[0, 0, -0.17]^{\top}\,\mathrm{m}$, with the shaft axis directed along $-z_{\mathrm{mid}} = [0, 0, -1]^{\top}$ toward \texttt{club\_shaft} ($[0, 0, -0.05]^{\top}\,\mathrm{m}$) and \texttt{club\_head} ($[0, 0, -0.55]^{\top}\,\mathrm{m}$).
To make both hands independently addressable without altering existing single-frame consumers, \texttt{export\_urdf(..., hands="separate")}, \texttt{SimOptions(hands="separate")}, and \texttt{build\_golfer\_model(hands="separate")} attach two massless body frames---\texttt{lead\_hand} and \texttt{trail\_hand}---welded to \texttt{mid\_hands} on parent joint \texttt{thorax2\_to\_thorax3} while retaining \texttt{mid\_hands} unchanged. With the default \texttt{hands="merged"}, the exported URDF bytes, joint counts ($n_q = 43$, $n_v = 43$, $n_{\mathrm{frames}} = 96$), link masses/inertias, frame table, and forward kinematics of every frame are byte- and numerically identical to the baseline.

Let $\Delta x_{\mathrm{hands}} = x_0(\mathrm{trail}) - x_0(\mathrm{lead}) = 0.0762\,\mathrm{m}$ be the axial hand spacing from \texttt{ContactTopology} (\texttt{hand\_x\_origin}), matching the shaft-axis separation of the left and right grip frames in \texttt{GripInterface}. Rotating from the canonical grip basis ($+x_{\mathrm{grip}}$ along the shaft from butt to clubhead) into \texttt{mid\_hands} ($-z_{\mathrm{mid}}$ along the shaft) via a $+\pi/2$ pitch about $+y$,
\begin{equation}
R_{\mathrm{mid}\leftarrow\mathrm{grip}} = R_y\!\left(\tfrac{\pi}{2}\right) =
\begin{bmatrix}
0 & 0 & 1 \\
0 & 1 & 0 \\
-1 & 0 & 0
\end{bmatrix}, \qquad
p_{\mathrm{mid},\mathrm{lead}} = \begin{bmatrix} 0 \\ 0 \\ +\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta x_{\mathrm{hands}} \end{bmatrix}, \qquad
p_{\mathrm{mid},\mathrm{trail}} = \begin{bmatrix} 0 \\ 0 \\ -\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta x_{\mathrm{hands}} \end{bmatrix},
\end{equation}
places \texttt{lead\_hand} and \texttt{trail\_hand} symmetrically about \texttt{mid\_hands} with Euclidean separation $\|p_{\mathrm{trail}} - p_{\mathrm{lead}}\|_2 = \Delta x_{\mathrm{hands}} = 0.0762\,\mathrm{m}$ (within $0\,\mathrm{mm}$ of the topology hand spacing) and zero added mass or inertia, so open-loop forward dynamics ($\mathrm{ABA}$) are unchanged.

\textbf{Capabilities, Manifest Resolution, and Allocated Reporting Evaluation.}
\texttt{NativePinocchioGolferGripAdapter} (\path{src/engines/physics_engines/pinocchio/python/native_golfer_grip_adapter.py}) implements \texttt{ModelGripAdapter} over the \texttt{hands="separate"} model:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,noitemsep]
\item \textit{Capabilities and Physical-Mode Refusal}: Because \texttt{lead\_hand}, \texttt{trail\_hand}, and \texttt{club\_shaft} are all fixed (\texttt{type="fixed"}) to \texttt{mid\_hands} on parent joint \texttt{thorax2\_to\_thorax3}, equal-and-opposite hand--club wrenches would cancel on \texttt{thorax2\_to\_thorax3}. Accordingly, \texttt{capabilities()} declares \texttt{attachment\_modes = ("weld",)}, \texttt{supports\_physical\_pairs = False}, \texttt{supports\_wrench\_application = True}, \texttt{native\_contact = False}, and \texttt{unavailable\_reason} naming the shared rigid joint \texttt{thorax2\_to\_thorax3}. Requesting any physical contact mode (\texttt{"contact"}, \texttt{"contact\_v1"}, \texttt{"separated\_v2"}) raises \texttt{SchemaFeatureUnavailableError} naming \texttt{thorax2\_to\_thorax3}.
\item \textit{Binding Resolution}: \texttt{resolve\_binding(topology)} validates \path{src/config/grip_bindings/pinocchio_native_golfer.json} (\texttt{schema = "grip-binding/v1"}, \texttt{attachment\_mode = "weld"}, \texttt{club\_body = "club\_shaft"}, \texttt{lead\_hand\_body = "lead\_hand"}, \texttt{trail\_hand\_body = "trail\_hand"}) via \texttt{binding\_from\_manifest}, \texttt{validate\_resolved\_binding}, and \texttt{validate\_binding}, resolving $100\%$ of topology sites across \texttt{overlap}, \texttt{interlock}, and \texttt{ten\_finger} (with and without \texttt{trigger\_finger}) using the club-local grip transforms $T_{\mathrm{shaft},\sigma} = ({}^{\mathcal{W}}T_{\mathrm{shaft}})^{-1}\,{}^{\mathcal{W}}T_{\sigma}$ for $\sigma \in \{\mathrm{lead}, \mathrm{trail}\}$.
\item \textit{Kinematic State Sampling and Allocated Pair Reporting}: \texttt{sample\_states(binding)} calls \texttt{pinocchio.forwardKinematics(model, data, q, v)} and \texttt{pinocchio.framesForwardKinematics(model, data, q)} to extract $4\times 4$ world poses ${}^{\mathcal{W}}T_B = \texttt{data.oMf}[f_B]$ and queries \texttt{pinocchio.getFrameVelocity(model, data, f\_B, LOCAL\_WORLD\_ALIGNED)} for world-aligned linear and angular velocities $(v_B, \omega_B)$. In \texttt{attachment\_mode = "weld"}, \texttt{evaluate\_contacts} evaluates the hand/club pad pairs kinematically at those FK states as reporting-only channels labelled \texttt{pair\_labels[p] = "allocated"} and \texttt{allocated\_labels[p] = "external:" + p + "\_allocated"} (consistent with HRG-H1 and HRG-GS-G3).
\item \textit{Wrench Application Guard}: In \texttt{attachment\_mode = "weld"}, \texttt{pair\_wrenches} and \texttt{body\_wrenches()} are empty ($\texttt{applied\_allocated\_force\_n} = 0.0\,\mathrm{N}$), so allocated channels never enter \texttt{apply\_wrenches}. Furthermore, \texttt{apply\_wrenches} refuses (raises \texttt{ValueError} naming \texttt{thorax2\_to\_thorax3}) any physical pair wrench targeting bodies that share a single parent joint, preventing a cancelling no-op from passing silently. On a $0.04\,\mathrm{s}$ hold trajectory on the $2\,\mathrm{ms}$ grid, the adapter's kinematic reporting series matches \texttt{law\_level\_series(..., mode="weld")} in \texttt{compare\_topology\_series} (\texttt{status = "passed"}, with every hand--grip pad pair verified in \texttt{allocated\_channels} within the $5\%$ peak and $2\%$ RMS tolerances), while physical-mode reference comparison reports \texttt{status = "unavailable"}.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Limitations.}
In the native Pinocchio golfer URDF, \texttt{lead\_hand}, \texttt{trail\_hand}, and \texttt{club\_shaft} are rigidly welded to \texttt{mid\_hands} on shared joint \texttt{thorax2\_to\_thorax3}; equal-and-opposite physical hand--club pair wrenches would cancel in net generalized torque on \texttt{thorax2\_to\_thorax3}, which is the reason \texttt{supports\_physical\_pairs = False} is declared, only \texttt{attachment\_modes = ("weld",)} is supported, and hand--club and hand--hand pairs are reporting-only allocated channels until the weld is relaxed into independent wrist/club degrees of freedom.

\textbf{Reproduction Command.}
\begin{verbatim}
python3 -m pytest tests/unit/grip_contact/test_pinocchio_native_golfer_grip.py
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{Native Drake Golfer Grip Adapter (HRG-GS-M10, \#13332)}
\textbf{Bilateral Attachment Topology and Parameters.}
In the canonical Drake golf humanoid (\path{src/engines/physics_engines/drake/models/generated/golfer.urdf}, loaded via \path{src/engines/physics_engines/drake/python/motion_matching/humanoid_urdf.py}), \texttt{club\_shaft} is welded to the trail hand (\texttt{right\_hand}) at local offset $p_{\mathrm{weld}} = (0, 0, -0.10)\,\mathrm{m}$ via fixed joint \texttt{grip\_lead}, leaving the lead hand (\texttt{left\_hand}) disconnected by default (\texttt{attachment="unilateral"}). Because the torso, right arm, club, and left arm form a closed kinematic loop, \texttt{load\_humanoid\_into\_plant(..., attachment="bilateral")} attaches \texttt{left\_hand} to \texttt{club\_shaft} using a compliant \texttt{LinearBushingRollPitchYaw} force element (never a second tree joint, leaving \texttt{num\_positions()} $= 35$, \texttt{num\_velocities()} $= 34$, and \texttt{golfer.urdf} byte-identical). The trail-hand grip anchor on \texttt{club\_shaft} sits at $p_{\mathrm{trail}}^{\mathrm{club}} = (0, 0, +0.10)\,\mathrm{m}$, and the lead-hand grip frame \texttt{club\_lead\_grip} is placed at the axial offset from \texttt{hand\_x\_origin} (\path{src/shared/python/grip_contact/topology_contact.py}):
\begin{equation}
\Delta x_{\mathrm{lead}} = x_{\mathrm{origin}}(\mathcal{T}, \mathrm{lead}) - x_{\mathrm{origin}}(\mathcal{T}, \mathrm{trail}) = -0.0725\,\mathrm{m} - 0.0000\,\mathrm{m} = -0.0725\,\mathrm{m}, \qquad
p_{\mathrm{lead}}^{\mathrm{club}} = \begin{bmatrix} \Delta x_{\mathrm{lead}} \\ 0 \\ 0.10 \end{bmatrix}\,\mathrm{m}.
\end{equation}
The bushing reuses the shared \texttt{BushingParameters} (\texttt{default\_bushing()} in \path{src/shared/python/grip_contact/parameters.py}):
\begin{equation}
k_{\mathrm{trans}} = (8000, 8000, 8000)\,\mathrm{N/m}, \quad
c_{\mathrm{trans}} = (160, 160, 160)\,\mathrm{N\cdot s/m}, \quad
k_{\mathrm{rot}} = (80, 150, 150)\,\mathrm{N\cdot m/rad}, \quad
c_{\mathrm{rot}} = (1.6, 3.0, 3.0)\,\mathrm{N\cdot m\cdot s/rad}.
\end{equation}
Over a $0.04\,\mathrm{s}$ prescribed hold at the bilateral IK address pose, the maximum world-space gap between \texttt{left\_hand\_grip} and \texttt{club\_lead\_grip} is $9.73 \times 10^{-6}\,\mathrm{m}$ ($0.0097\,\mathrm{mm} < 1.0\,\mathrm{mm}$).

\textbf{Adapter Equations and Contact Normal Projection.}
\texttt{NativeDrakeGolferGripAdapter} (\path{src/engines/physics_engines/drake/python/native_golfer_grip_adapter.py}) resolves its \texttt{HandClubBinding} from \path{src/config/grip_bindings/drake_native_golfer.json} (\texttt{grip-binding/v1}), samples body poses ${}^{W}T_{B}$ and spatial velocities $(v_{B,W}, \omega_{B,W})$ from the Drake \texttt{MultibodyPlant} context, and applies physical body wrenches $(F_{B,W}, M_{B,W})$ as \texttt{ExternallyAppliedSpatialForce} entries about body origins $p_{BoBq,B} = 0$ on \texttt{plant.get\_applied\_spatial\_force\_input\_port()}. Zero native collision geometries are registered on the grip pairs (\texttt{registered\_grip\_collision\_count()} $= 0$), ensuring contact is never applied twice.
Following the G0 Drake frame convention, let $\hat{n}_{BA,W}$ be the unit contact normal pointing from body $B$ (\texttt{club\_shaft}) toward body $A$ (hand site) in world axes, so the inward normal into the club is $\hat{n}_{\mathrm{in}} = -\hat{n}_{BA,W}$. The recorded contact normal force $f_n$ and tangential friction magnitude $f_t$ (\texttt{project\_pair\_contact\_normal}) are:
\begin{equation}
f_n = \max\!\left(0,\, F_{b,W} \cdot (-\hat{n}_{BA,W})\right), \qquad
f_t = \left\| F_{b,W} - f_n\,(-\hat{n}_{BA,W}) \right\|_2.
\end{equation}
Under pure tangential axial slip ($v_{\mathrm{slip}} = 0.05\,\hat{e}_{x,\mathrm{club}}\,\mathrm{m/s}$, orthogonal to $\hat{n}_{BA,W}$), $f_n$ remains invariant to $\le 10^{-12}\,\mathrm{N}$.

\textbf{Per-Hand Capability Table and Physics Honesty.}
Per the physics-honesty rule, \texttt{supports\_physical\_pairs} is \texttt{True} only for a hand that is a distinct rigid body from \texttt{club\_shaft} and is not welded to it (\texttt{plant.GetBodiesWeldedTo(club\_body)}). Because \texttt{right\_hand} is welded to \texttt{club\_shaft}, its hand--club pairs act within a single rigid body and are reporting/allocated only; calling \texttt{apply\_wrenches} or \texttt{apply\_pair\_wrench} on \texttt{right\_hand} or any welded pair raises \texttt{ValueError} rather than silently cancelling. Under the bilateral bushing, \texttt{left\_hand} is a distinct unwelded body, so its pad pairs can act physically in parallel with the bushing:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{llllll}
\toprule
Role / Pair Group & Model Body & Kinematic Relation to \texttt{club\_shaft} & \texttt{supports\_physical\_pairs} & \texttt{supports\_wrench\_application} & Force Application Policy \\
\midrule
Lead hand (\texttt{L}) & \texttt{left\_hand} & Distinct body (\texttt{LinearBushingRollPitchYaw}) & \texttt{True} & \texttt{True} & Physical pad wrenches in parallel with bushing \\
Trail hand (\texttt{R}) & \texttt{right\_hand} & Welded (\texttt{grip\_lead} fixed joint) & \texttt{False} & \texttt{False} & Reporting/allocated only; raises \texttt{ValueError} if applied \\
Hand--hand pairs & \texttt{left\_hand}--\texttt{right\_hand} & Cross-hand overlap/interlock/abutment & \texttt{False} & \texttt{False} & Allocated (\texttt{NaN} + reason); $0.0\,\mathrm{N}$ applied \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\textbf{M3 Qualification Parity Table.}
Across all three grip styles on the $0.04\,\mathrm{s}$ prescribed hold ($1100\,\mathrm{N}$ squeeze per hand, $\Delta t = 2\,\mathrm{ms}$), \texttt{compare\_topology\_series} passes under the unchanged $5\%$ peak and $2\%$ RMS tolerances with zero applied allocated force (\texttt{applied\_allocated\_force\_n} $= 0.0\,\mathrm{N}$):
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
\toprule
Grip Style & Plant Hold vs.\ \texttt{law\_level\_series} & Plant Hold vs.\ \texttt{PinocchioGripAdapter} & Dynamic Hold Peak Error vs.\ Pinocchio & Dynamic Hold RMS Error vs.\ Pinocchio & Verdict \\
\midrule
\texttt{overlap} & $0.0000\%$ peak / $0.0000\%$ RMS & $0.0000\%$ peak / $0.0000\%$ RMS & $0.2733\%$ ($\le 5.0\%$) & $0.5090\%$ ($\le 2.0\%$) & \texttt{passed} \\
\texttt{interlock} & $0.0000\%$ peak / $0.0000\%$ RMS & $0.0000\%$ peak / $0.0000\%$ RMS & $0.2733\%$ ($\le 5.0\%$) & $0.5090\%$ ($\le 2.0\%$) & \texttt{passed} \\
\texttt{ten\_finger} & $0.0000\%$ peak / $0.0000\%$ RMS & $0.0000\%$ peak / $0.0000\%$ RMS & $0.2996\%$ ($\le 5.0\%$) & $0.3001\%$ ($\le 2.0\%$) & \texttt{passed} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\textbf{Limitations.}
Rigid hand segments (\texttt{representation="rigid\_proxy"}) carry fixed contact-site proxies without finger articulation or intrinsic muscle recruitment. Because \texttt{right\_hand} is welded to \texttt{club\_shaft} in the canonical URDF, trail-hand pad forces act within one rigid body and are reporting/allocated only; lead-hand pad forces under \texttt{attachment="bilateral"} act in parallel with the compliant \texttt{LinearBushingRollPitchYaw}. Hand--hand pairs are allocated equivalents (\texttt{NaN} plus structured reason when no closure wrench is supplied) and never enter force application.
