python3 -m pytest tests/unit/grip_contact/test_drake_native_golfer_grip.py
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{Array-Namespace Contact Kernel (HRG-GS-M14, \#13336)}
\textbf{Kernel Equations and Sign Conventions.}
The module \path{src/shared/python/motion_matching/contact_kernel.py} holds the single implementation of the qualified Hunt-Crossley normal, regularised Coulomb--Stribeck tangential, and torsional spin-friction law in \texttt{contact\_law\_kernel(penetration\_m, normal\_velocity\_m\_s, tangential\_velocity\_m\_s, params, spin\_rate\_rad\_s=0.0, *, xp=np)} and \texttt{torsional\_moment\_kernel}. Given penetration depth $\delta$ ($\mathrm{m}$, positive when overlapping, $\le 0$ when separated), normal relative velocity $v_n = \hat{n} \cdot v_{\mathrm{rel}} = -\dot{\delta}$ ($\mathrm{m/s}$, negative when approaching/deepening), tangential slip velocity $v_t$ ($\mathrm{m/s}$, scalar of shape $S$ or 3-vector in the contact plane of shape $(S, 3)$), normal relative spin rate $\omega_n = \hat{n} \cdot \omega_{\mathrm{rel}}$ ($\mathrm{rad/s}$), and parameter tuple $\theta = (k, c, \mu_s, \mu_d, \mu_v, v_{\mathrm{tr}}, r_{\mathrm{patch}}, \omega_{\mathrm{tr}})$ (\texttt{ContactKernelParams}), the kernel evaluates:
\begin{align}
\delta_+ &= \operatorname{where}(\delta > 0,\, \delta,\, 0), \qquad
\tilde{f}_n = k\,\delta_+\,(1 - c\,v_n), \qquad
f_n = \operatorname{where}\!\left((\delta > 0) \land (\tilde{f}_n > 0),\, \tilde{f}_n,\, 0\right), \\
s &= \|v_t\|_2, \qquad
\mu(s) = \mu_d + (\mu_s - \mu_d)\exp\!\left(-\left(\frac{s}{v_{\mathrm{tr}}}\right)^2\right) + \mu_v s, \\
f_t &= \operatorname{where}\!\left((s > 0) \land (f_n > 0),\, \mu(s)\,f_n\,\tanh\!\left(\frac{s}{v_{\mathrm{tr}}}\right),\, 0\right), \qquad
\vec{f}_t = \operatorname{where}\!\left((s > 0) \land (f_n > 0),\, -\frac{v_t}{s_{\mathrm{safe}}}\,f_t,\, \vec{0}\right), \\
m_s &= \operatorname{where}\!\left(f_n > 0,\, -\mu_s\,f_n\,r_{\mathrm{patch}}\,\tanh\!\left(\frac{\omega_n}{\omega_{\mathrm{tr}}}\right),\, 0\right).
\end{align}
Here $s_{\mathrm{safe}} = \operatorname{where}(s^2 > 0, \sqrt{\operatorname{where}(s^2 > 0, s^2, 1)}, 1)$ applies the double-\texttt{xp.where} idiom so that both value evaluation and reverse-mode automatic differentiation (\texttt{jax.grad}, \texttt{jax.jacrev}) remain free of $0/0$ and $1/(2\sqrt{0})$ \texttt{NaN} propagation on the unselected branch at $v_t = 0$.

\textbf{Single Kernel Callers, Scalar Fast Path, and Validation Split.}
All NumPy and JAX contact-law entry points delegate to this single kernel:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,noitemsep]
\item \path{src/shared/python/motion_matching/contact_law.py}: \texttt{sphere\_ground\_contact} calls \texttt{contact\_law\_kernel(..., xp=np)} and \texttt{torsional\_friction\_moment} calls \texttt{torsional\_moment\_kernel(..., xp=np)}.
\item \path{src/shared/python/motion_matching/jax_contact.py}: \texttt{sphere\_ground\_contact\_jax} calls \texttt{contact\_law\_kernel(..., xp=jnp)}.
\item \path{src/shared/python/grip_contact/pair_contact.py} and \path{pad_contact.py}: call \texttt{sphere\_ground\_contact} and \texttt{torsional\_friction\_moment} in \path{contact_law.py}, reaching the same kernel without maintaining a separate formula in \path{grip_contact}.
\end{itemize}
When \texttt{xp is np} and scalar kinematics are supplied, the kernel binds \texttt{ops} to \texttt{\_ScalarMathOps} (backed by Python \texttt{math.exp}, \texttt{math.tanh}, \texttt{math.sqrt}, and ternary \texttt{where}) inside the same single-formula body, avoiding $0$-D \texttt{ndarray} ufunc overhead on the hot scalar path ($100\text{k}$ calls, best of $5$: $5.9685\,\mathrm{s}$ pre-refactor vs.\ $5.6782\,\mathrm{s}$ post-refactor, $0.951\times$, compared with $9.3722\,\mathrm{s}$ / $1.48\times$ when routing scalars through $0$-D NumPy ufuncs). Design-by-Contract validation executes outside the traced kernel in \texttt{validate\_kernel\_params}, \texttt{evaluate\_contact\_kernel}, \texttt{evaluate\_contact\_kernel\_jax}, \texttt{sphere\_ground\_contact}, \texttt{torsional\_friction\_moment}, and \texttt{sphere\_ground\_contact\_jax}. \texttt{jax.numpy} is imported lazily inside \texttt{evaluate\_contact\_kernel\_jax} and \texttt{jax\_contact\_kernel} so cold imports of \texttt{motion\_matching} and \texttt{grip\_contact} never load \texttt{jax}.

\textbf{Non-Differentiable Loci and Opt-In Smooth Relaxation.}
The qualified law is preserved without silent smoothing; its three non-smooth regimes are:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,noitemsep]
\item \textit{Contact Onset at Zero Penetration ($\delta = 0$) and Damping Liftoff ($v_n = 1/c$)}: The unilateral hinge $\max(0, \delta)$ has left derivative $\partial_- f_n / \partial \delta = 0$ and right derivative $\partial_+ f_n / \partial \delta = k(1 - c\,v_n) > 0$ at $\delta = 0$ (and $\partial_\pm f_n / \partial v_n$ jumps from $-k c \delta$ to $0$ at $v_n = 1/c$).
\item \textit{Zero Tangential Speed ($v_t = 0$)}: The Euclidean speed $s = \|v_t\|_2$ has a $C^0$ cone singularity at $v_t = 0$, causing the tangential force magnitude $f_t(v_t)$ to have opposite one-sided derivatives $\partial_\pm f_t / \partial v_t|_{0^\pm} = \pm \mu_s f_n / v_{\mathrm{tr}}$.
\item \textit{Stick/Slip Transition ($|v_t| \sim v_{\mathrm{tr}}$)}: Across the boundary layer $s \approx v_{\mathrm{tr}}$ ($10^{-3}\,\mathrm{m/s}$), the tangent modulus $\partial f_t / \partial s$ collapses by $> 10^3\times$ from the stick stiffness $\mu_s f_n / v_{\mathrm{tr}}$ ($s \ll v_{\mathrm{tr}}$) to the viscous slip slope $\mu_v f_n$ ($s \gg v_{\mathrm{tr}}$), causing finite-difference steps of scale $h \sim v_{\mathrm{tr}}$ across the knee to incur $O(1)$ relative error ($> 10\%$).
\end{enumerate}
For gradient-based trajectory optimization requiring $C^\infty$ derivatives across $\delta = 0$ and $v_t = 0$, the separately named function \texttt{smooth\_contact\_law\_kernel} replaces $\max(0, x)$ with $\frac{1}{2}(x + \sqrt{x^2 + \varepsilon_\delta^2})$ and $\|v_t\|_2$ with $\sqrt{v_t \cdot v_t + \varepsilon_v^2} - \varepsilon_v$, converging to \texttt{contact\_law\_kernel} as $(\varepsilon_\delta, \varepsilon_v) \to (0^+, 0^+)$ and never replacing the default law.

\textbf{Measured Equivalence, Parity, and Gradient Errors.}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,noitemsep]
\item Pre-refactor oracle equivalence ($\le 10^{-12}$ gate across $500$ seeded states including zero-penetration, zero-speed, and damping-liftoff edges): maximum absolute error is $0.0\,\mathrm{N}$ on \texttt{sphere\_ground\_contact} normal force, $0.0\,\mathrm{N}$ on friction force, and $1.14 \times 10^{-13}\,\mathrm{N\cdot m}$ on \texttt{torsional\_friction\_moment}.
\item JAX vs.\ NumPy \texttt{float64} value parity ($\le 10^{-10}$ gate across $200$ seeded states): maximum absolute error is $0.0\,\mathrm{N}$ on $f_n$, $5.68 \times 10^{-14}\,\mathrm{N}$ on scalar $f_t$, $1.14 \times 10^{-13}\,\mathrm{N}$ on 3-vector $\vec{f}_t$, and $8.88 \times 10^{-16}\,\mathrm{N\cdot m}$ on $m_s$.
\item Smooth-region gradient parity vs.\ central differences ($h = 10^{-7}$, $\le 10^{-5}$ gate across $25$ seeded states): maximum relative $\ell_2$ gradient error is $5.92 \times 10^{-12}$ on $\nabla f_n$, $8.97 \times 10^{-9}$ on $\nabla f_t$, and $9.78 \times 10^{-12}$ on $\nabla m_s$.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Limitations.}
The kernel evaluates point/patch contact forces from local penetration and relative velocity; multi-body contact geometry (closest-point queries on capsules and cylinders) remains in the calling geometry layer. Rigid-proxy hand bindings do not identify biological finger articulation or co-contraction squeeze.

\textbf{Reproduction Command.}
\begin{verbatim}
JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu python3 -m pytest tests/unit/grip_contact/test_contact_kernel.py \
    tests/unit/motion_matching/test_contact_law.py \
    tests/unit/motion_matching/test_jax_contact.py
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{Simscape Grip Topology Export and Allocated Reporting (HRG-GS-M11, \#13333)}
\textbf{Model Topology, Manifest, and Sibling Export.}
Simscape Multibody (\path{GolfSwing3D_Human.slx}, model ID \texttt{simscape\_gs3dx\_human}) models the two-handed grip as a closed kinematic loop with welded hand--club constraints (\texttt{attachment\_mode="weld"} in \path{src/config/grip_bindings/simscape_gs3dx_human.json}, schema \texttt{grip-binding/v1}). Rather than modifying \path{gs3dx_appearance.v1.json}---which remains $100\%$ byte-identical for v1 consumers---Python generates a deterministic sibling file \path{gs3dx_grip_topology.json} (\texttt{schema\_version="simscape-grip-topology/v1"}) via \path{scripts/export_simscape_grip_topology.py} (with \texttt{--check} drift detection). The export serializes:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,noitemsep]
\item All six canonical \texttt{ContactTopology} variants ($\{\texttt{overlap}, \texttt{interlock}, \texttt{ten\_finger}\} \times \{\texttt{trigger\_finger} \in \{\text{false}, \text{true}\}\}$), preserving $100\%$ of site identifiers, primitive geometries (sphere and capsule radii, endpoints, normals, tangents), and contact pair identifiers (\texttt{hand\_grip\_pair\_ids} and \texttt{hand\_hand\_pair\_ids}), with every pair explicitly tagged \texttt{simscape\_label="allocated"}.
\item Posed G4 hand mesh geometry from \texttt{build\_grip\_hand\_mesh} (\path{src/shared/python/model_appearance/grip_hand_mesh.py}) for both lead and trail hands across each style variant, including palm/thumb/phalanx components, $V$-azimuths, thumb azimuths, axial distances below the grip butt ($0.065$\,m lead, $0.145$\,m trail), and 3D fingertip coordinates in the shared grip frame.
\item Named grip presets (\texttt{palmer\_overlap}, \texttt{woods\_interlock}, \texttt{ten\_finger}) with \texttt{photo\_estimate} provenance.
\end{enumerate}
On the MATLAB side, \path{gs3dx_read_grip_topology.m} acts strictly as a thin JSON reader (\texttt{jsondecode} + schema check) with zero re-implementation of contact topology or Hunt--Crossley/Coulomb contact laws.

\textbf{Coordinate Frames, Units, and Parameters.}
All exported and allocated quantities use SI units ($\mathrm{m}$, $\mathrm{rad}$, $\mathrm{N}$, $\mathrm{N\cdot m}$). In the shared grip frame $F_{\mathrm{grip}}$, $+x$ points from grip butt toward clubhead, $+y$ toward the target line, and $+z = +x \times +y$. Grip cylinder radius is $r_{\mathrm{grip}} = 0.014$\,m and default baseline hand squeeze is $F_{\mathrm{sq}} = 120.0$\,N per hand.

\textbf{Equations and Numerical Method (Allocated Reporting from Recorded States).}
Given a recorded closed-loop state snapshot $s_k$ from a \texttt{simscape-closed-loop-snapshot/v1} export ($\mathbf{M}\mathbf{a} + \mathbf{h} = \mathbf{S}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\tau} + \mathbf{J}_c^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_c$), the closure constraint multiplier $\boldsymbol{\lambda}_c \in \mathbb{R}^m$ is obtained via \texttt{solve\_closure\_multiplier} and lifted into the canonical 6-DOF grip-frame closure wrench $\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{grip}} = [\mathbf{f}_c^{\top}, \boldsymbol{\tau}_c^{\top}]^{\top} \in \mathbb{R}^6$ at the grip origin:
\begin{equation}
\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{grip}} = \mathcal{L}_{\text{closure}}\!\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_c\right), \quad
\mathbf{f}_c = [f_{c,x}, f_{c,y}, f_{c,z}]^{\top}, \quad
\boldsymbol{\tau}_c = [\tau_{c,x}, \tau_{c,y}, \tau_{c,z}]^{\top}.
\end{equation}
All contact forces in the welded Simscape loop are reporting-only allocations (\texttt{label="allocated (uniform prior, welded hands)"}) with identically zero injected forces:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,noitemsep]
\item \textit{Hand--Hand Allocation (G3)}: \texttt{allocate\_hand\_hand\_from\_closure}$(\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{grip}}, \mathcal{T}, \text{prior})$ solves the minimum-weighted-norm linear system $\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{hh}}\mathbf{f}_{\mathrm{hh}} = \mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{grip}}$ over the active hand--hand pairs $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{hh}}(\mathcal{T})$, reporting per-pair normal force $f_{n,i} = \max(0, -\mathbf{n}_i \cdot \mathbf{f}_i)$, tangential magnitude $f_{t,i} = \|\mathbf{f}_i - (\mathbf{f}_i \cdot \mathbf{n}_i)\mathbf{n}_i\|_2$, and spin moment $m_{s,i}$.
\item \textit{Palm--Thumb Legacy Decomposition (H1)}: \texttt{allocate\_from\_closure}$(\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{grip}})$ decomposes $\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{grip}}$ across the trail-thenar / lead-thumb and finger-saddle contact patches.
\item \textit{Hand--Grip Pad Allocation}: For active hand--grip pad pairs $p \in \mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{hg}}(\mathcal{T})$ on hand $\sigma \in \{\text{lead}, \text{trail}\}$ with pad weight $w_p > 0$ ($\sum_{p \in \mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{hg},\sigma}} w_p = W_\sigma$), normal force combines baseline radial squeeze $F_{\mathrm{sq}}$ and radial closure projection, while tangential force and spin moment distribute axial/transverse shear and shaft torque:
\begin{align}
f_{n,p} &= \frac{w_p}{W_\sigma} F_{\mathrm{sq}} + \frac{w_p}{\sum_{q \in \mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{hg}}} w_q} \max\!\left(0, -\mathbf{n}_p \cdot \mathbf{f}_c\right), \\
f_{t,p} &= \frac{w_p}{\sum_{q \in \mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{hg}}} w_q} \left( \|\mathbf{f}_c - (\mathbf{f}_c \cdot \mathbf{n}_p)\mathbf{n}_p\|_2 + \frac{\sqrt{\tau_{c,y}^2 + \tau_{c,z}^2}}{\max(r_p, r_{\mathrm{grip}})} \right), \quad
m_{s,p} = \frac{w_p}{\sum_{q \in \mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{hg}}} w_q} |\tau_{c,x}|.
\end{align}
\item \textit{Zero Injected Wrench Invariant}: Every pair in the resulting \texttt{GripStepReport} carries \texttt{pair\_labels}$[p] = \texttt{"allocated"}$, so \texttt{body\_wrenches()} returns $\mathbf{0} \in \mathbb{R}^3$ for all bodies, \texttt{injected\_body\_wrenches} is empty, and \texttt{SimscapeGripReportingAdapter.apply\_wrenches} raises \texttt{SchemaFeatureUnavailableError} if called.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Acceptance Criteria and Deferred R2025b Validation.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,noitemsep]
\item Schema validation passes for \path{gs3dx_grip_topology.json} and two consecutive runs produce identical SHA-256 digests; \path{gs3dx_appearance.v1.json} remains byte-identical.
\item $100\%$ of \texttt{ContactTopology} pair IDs and site IDs are preserved across all six style/trigger variants, and exported fingertip positions match G4 \texttt{build\_grip\_hand\_mesh} to $\le 10^{-8}$\,m.
\item Allocated reporting on synthetic \texttt{WELD6} and checked-in planar \texttt{simscape-closed-loop-snapshot/v1} fixtures produces finite non-negative pad forces, hand--hand wrench reconstruction error $\le 10^{-9}$\,N, and zero injected forces ($\texttt{injected\_force\_count} = 0$, $\max \|\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{inj}}\| = 0$).
\item Native MATLAB R2025b execution of \texttt{gs3dx\_read\_grip\_topology} and Mechanics Explorer visual verification on \path{GolfSwing3D_Human.slx} is recorded as deferred validation \texttt{HRG-GS-M11} (\#13333) in \path{docs/development/deferred-catalog-bundle.json}, explicitly targeting \path{C:/Program Files/MATLAB/R2025b/bin/matlab.exe}.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Limitations.}
Because Simscape \path{GolfSwing3D_Human.slx} welds the hands into a closed kinematic loop rather than stepping compliant 3D contact dynamics at every phalanx, all reported pad and inter-hand forces are post-hoc allocations from the loop-closure reaction wrench under a regularizing prior, not dynamically identified biological finger forces.

\textbf{Reproduction Command.}
\begin{verbatim}
python3 scripts/export_simscape_grip_topology.py --check
python3 scripts/export_simscape_appearance.py --check
python3 -m pytest tests/unit/grip_contact/test_simscape_grip_topology_export.py
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{Character Builder Grip Binding Admission (HRG-GS-M13, \#13335)}
When the humanoid character builder and multi-engine exporters compose a golf
club onto the anthropometric golfer topology, they emit and validate a
\texttt{grip-binding/v1} manifest via
\texttt{src/shared/python/grip\_contact/binding\_manifest.py} (reusing the M2
schema without introducing a parallel format):
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Exporters wired}: \texttt{spec\_export.build\_grip\_binding\_manifest}
and \texttt{spec\_export.build\_summary} (embedding \texttt{grip\_binding} in
summary metadata), \texttt{model\_bundle.export\_model\_bundle} and
\texttt{export\_full\_body\_urdf} (embedding \texttt{grip\_binding} in
\texttt{sidecar.json}), \texttt{full\_body\_mjcf.export\_full\_body\_mjcf}
(embedding \texttt{grip\_binding} in returned metadata),
\texttt{CharacterBuilderModel.export} (writing \texttt{<stem>.grip\_binding.json}
next to exported URDF/MJCF/spec artifacts), and
\texttt{model\_converter.urdf\_exporter} (\texttt{build\_canonical\_grip\_binding\_manifest}
for \texttt{golfer\_canonical.yaml} when \texttt{club\_shaft}, \texttt{hand\_left},
and \texttt{hand\_right} are composed).
\item \textbf{Fail-closed clubless contract}: Any clubless export path that
requests grip binding (\texttt{require\_grip\_binding=True} on a clubless
\texttt{full-body-v1} spec, standalone clubless \texttt{HumanoidURDFGenerator},
or clubless \texttt{CanonicalModel}) raises
\texttt{SchemaFeatureUnavailableError} with a non-empty \texttt{reason}
attribute through \texttt{fail\_closed\_grip\_binding}; empty manifests are
never emitted.
\item \textbf{Model-library admission}: Every advertised hand-bearing asset
enumerated by \texttt{enumerate\_advertised\_hand\_bearing\_assets} across
\texttt{src/config/engine\_model\_inventory.json}, \texttt{src/config/models.yaml}
launcher tiles, and \texttt{src/shared/python/model\_generation/library/bundled/manifest.json}
is verified by \texttt{verify\_model\_library\_admission} to be 100\,\% covered:
either \texttt{bound} via a validated manifest in
\texttt{src/config/grip\_bindings/} (including
\texttt{humanoid\_character\_builder.json} and
\texttt{full\_body\_v1\_spec\_export.json}) or \texttt{reason\_coded} in
\texttt{src/config/grip\_capability\_inventory.json} (including the bundled
clubless \texttt{mujoco\_humanoid} asset, while handless bundled assets
\texttt{simple\_humanoid}, \texttt{simple\_arm}, and \texttt{simple\_quadruped}
are recorded in \texttt{excluded}).
\item \textbf{Neutral preload}: At the neutral calibration pose $q_0$ (metres,
radians, shaft axis aligned with $+x$ in the grip frame), every bound pad site $i$
 across \texttt{overlap}, \texttt{interlock}, and \texttt{ten\_finger} (with and
without trigger finger) satisfies
\[
  \left|\delta_i(q_0) - \delta_0\right| \le 5.0\times 10^{-4}\text{ m},
  \qquad \delta_0 = 2.5\times 10^{-4}\text{ m},
\]
verified in shared geometry, on the exported URDF via Pinocchio forward
kinematics, and on the exported MJCF via MuJoCo forward kinematics.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Limitations.} The character builder exports rigid-hand proxy bodies
(\texttt{hand\_l}, \texttt{hand\_r}) rather than articulated 20-DOF phalanx
chains; articulated finger contact remains tracked in engine-specific child
issues.

\textbf{Reproduction.}
\begin{verbatim}
PYTHONPATH=.:src pytest -o addopts="" -q \
    tests/unit/humanoid_character_builder/test_character_builder_grip_binding.py \
    tests/unit/grip_contact/test_grip_capability_inventory.py \
    tests/unit/grip_contact/test_grip_binding_manifests.py
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{The \texttt{full-body-v2} Schema (HRG-D1, \#13170)}
The \texttt{full-body-v2} schema replaces the v1 trunk (\texttt{TorsoInput}
and \texttt{SpineInputX/Y}) with two regional 3-DOF joints, lumbar (L5/S1)
and thoracic (T12/L1), each ISB \texttt{isb\_spine} ZXY. Coordinates migrate
from v1 through a constrained rotation solve with the
\texttt{bruno2015\_regional} prior. The solve reports its residuals and its
non-uniqueness. \texttt{full-body-v1} stays the default. The definition,
equations and residual report are in the Simscape matching reference,
section ``The full-body-v2 Schema and Trunk Coordinate Migration''. The geometry
is HRG-D2.

\subsection{Landmark Regional Trunk Joints (HRG-D2, \#13173)}
HRG-D2 places L5/S1 at the HRG-C3 design offset and C7/T1 at the cervicale.
T12/L1 is placed at the Gray lumbar fraction $18/46$, and the neutral
curvature is checked against Yukawa et al.\ 2018. The de Leva trunk solids
are re-parented and the dummy body is dropped. The ROM becomes soft limits,
with hard ranges report-only. The back markers observe rank 5 of the six
trunk coordinates, so the split is regularised by
\texttt{bruno2015\_regional}. The details, the mass ledger, the cross-engine
results and the best-fit Hub residual (61.9 / 34.3~mm) are in the Simscape
matching reference, section ``Landmark Regional Trunk Joints for
full-body-v2''.

\subsection{Articulated Pelvis, Abdomen and Thorax Visuals (HRG-D3, \#13174)}
This slice uses three rigid visuals, one for each trunk segment. Skinned
blending is not used. The MuJoCo exporter now accepts the visual layer for
\texttt{full-body-v2}. Before this change it raised
\texttt{SchemaFeatureUnavailableError}. The pelvis, \texttt{trunk\_lumbar}
and thorax bodies each draw the \texttt{visual\_hints} ellipsoid of their own
de Leva solid (pelvis, middle\_trunk, upper\_trunk) and their own skeleton
capsules. Each visual is attached to its own MJCF body, so it follows the
lumbar and thoracic joints. In the appearance layer,
\texttt{trunk\_lumbar} is classified as \texttt{torso}, so the abdomen wears
the shirt.

The seam checks run in
\path{tests/unit/motion_matching/test_full_body_v2_trunk_visuals.py}. They
cover the driver and 7-iron captures. They use every corner of the soft-ROM
box of the six trunk coordinates (64 poses) and 64 seeded interior poses.
\begin{itemize}
\item No gap. Each regional joint centre stays strictly inside both adjacent
  ellipsoids. The normalised levels at $q=0$ (driver) are 0.375 and 0.759 at
  L5/S1, and 0.261 and 0.768 at T12/L1, where a level below 1 means inside.
  The joint centre is fixed in both bodies, so this holds at every pose.
\item No telescoping. A child ellipsoid centre never enters its parent's
  ellipsoid.
\item No pelvis--thorax interpenetration. Every sampled thorax surface point
  stays outside the pelvis ellipsoid.
\end{itemize}
A separate exploratory probe used the driver capture, the 64 corners, 200
random poses (seed 0) and 4000 surface points. Its minimum levels were 2.12
for the abdomen centre in the pelvis, 1.84 for the thorax centre in the
abdomen, and 2.47 for the thorax surface in the pelvis.
Adjacent ellipsoids do overlap locally at each seam. That overlap is what
closes the gap, so the test does not require zero adjacent overlap.

The visuals do not change the physics. The plain, visual and appearance
exports compile to identical body, joint, inertia and equality arrays. They
also give identical \texttt{xpos} and \texttt{qacc} at a seeded state.

Deferred: stills at address, top and impact, and a render check of the
visible thoracic rotation. The work machine has no GL context, so no render
evidence is claimed. Skinned blending across the lumbar and thoracic joints
(\path{skinned_body.py}) is also not done in this slice.

\section{Anatomical Angles}
\label{sec:angles}

\subsection{Wrist and Forearm Angles of \texttt{full-body-v1} (HRG-E2, \#13169)}
\paragraph{Package.}
\path{src/shared/python/anatomical_angles/} (PR \#13241) decomposes a
radius-to-hand rotation in the ISB wrist sequence (\texttt{isb\_wrist},
$ZXY$: flexion, ulnar deviation, axial rotation) and a humerus-to-forearm
rotation in the ISB elbow sequence (\texttt{isb\_elbow}: flexion, carrying
angle, pronation).  Forearm pronation is reported separately from the third
wrist angle.  Left-side raw angles are mapped by
$\mathrm{diag}(1,-1,-1)$, so flexion, ulnar deviation and pronation are
positive on both sides.  At $|\text{second angle}|=90^{\circ}$ only the first
and third components become NaN, each with a reason.  Every series carries
\texttt{observability = "fitted\_grip\_rotation"} and its receipt path.

\paragraph{Engine Adapter and Neutral Calibration.}
\path{anatomical_angles/full_body_v1.py} takes the world rotations of three
bodies per side from any engine.  Their names come from the sign map
(\S\ref{sec:signmap}): \texttt{LUpperArm}, \texttt{LLowerForearm},
\texttt{Clubface Vector} on the left, and \texttt{RUpperArm},
\texttt{RLowerForearm}, \texttt{RHandStandoff} on the right.  The
specification has no palm, thumb or styloid landmark, so the Wu frames are a
\emph{declared} calibration at $q=0$, using the HRG-00 convention of a
neutral forearm and wrist (\S\ref{sec:wrist}).  In the model basis:
\begin{itemize}[nosep]
  \item Wu wrist frame, with radius and hand coincident at $q=0$:
    $Y=-x$ (proximal).  $X$ (volar) is $-y$ on the left and $+y$ on the
    right.  $Z=X\times Y$ is then radial ($+z$) on the right and ulnar
    ($-z$) on the left.
  \item ISB elbow frame, the same on both sides, with humerus and forearm
    coincident at $q=0$: $X=+z$, $Y=-x$, $Z=-y$.  Pronation is measured from
    the neutral (thumb-up) forearm, not from ISB's supinated anatomical zero.
\end{itemize}
Each body gets a constant offset $C=R_{\mathrm{body}}(q{=}0)^{\top}W$, and
the anatomical frame is $R_{\mathrm{body}}(t)\,C$.  The offset absorbs the
engine's body axes and the declared physical grip rotation in the wrist base.
The hand frame therefore follows the physical hand or club body only.
Appearance and visual grip parameters are not inputs.  A native-basis spec
(no \texttt{WRIST\_ROLL}) is refused by \texttt{check\_spec\_family}.

\paragraph{Verification.}
\path{tests/unit/anatomical_angles/test_full_body_v1_wrist.py} checks the
following:
\begin{itemize}[nosep]
  \item On the compiled MuJoCo fixture spec, each arm coordinate at
    $+20^{\circ}$ gives the sign-map meaning to $10^{-6}$\,rad:
    \texttt{LWInputY} is extension and \texttt{RWInputY} is flexion;
    \texttt{*WInputX} is radial deviation; \texttt{RFInput} is pronation and
    \texttt{LFInput} is supination; \texttt{*EInput} is elbow extension.
  \item With a synthetic chain built from the pinned axes, every component is
    zero at $q=0$.
  \item The pronation coordinate leaves the wrist neutral.
  \item Mirrored motions give identical anatomical values.
  \item Arbitrary engine body axes and pelvis placement leave the angles
    unchanged to $10^{-10}$.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Best-Fit Values Pending.}
The private best-fit bundle (\S\ref{sec:model}) was not on the machine that
built this revision, so no best-fit wrist or forearm numbers appear here.
The report-only test prints the address value and the extremes per side when
it is given the bundle:
{\small
\begin{verbatim}
HRG_BEST_FIT_BUNDLE=<private-bundle> pytest -o addopts="" -q -n 0 -s \
  tests/unit/anatomical_angles/test_full_body_v1_wrist.py \
  -k best_fit_wrist_forearm_report
\end{verbatim}}
Because the wrist angles come from the fitted grip rotation, they are
model-estimated, not measured hand anatomy.

\subsection{Event Table and Export of Anatomical Angles (HRG-E4, \#13175)}
\paragraph{Scope of This Slice.}
\path{anatomical_angles/event_table.py} samples the wrist and forearm series
of HRG-E2 at the swing events address, top, impact and finish.  It does not
detect events.  The caller passes frame indices from an existing detector
(for example \texttt{swing\_comparison.events.detect\_events}) and names it
in \texttt{event\_source}, which every export records.  Frames must be in
range and non-decreasing in the order above.  Lead and trail sides follow
\texttt{core.handedness}.  A side without series gives no rows; nothing is
filled in.  Pelvis reversal and the trunk terms are not on \texttt{main} at
this revision (open PR \#13498), so this slice does not accept them.

\paragraph{Terms, Units and Signs.}
Term identifiers, frames and sign conventions are read from
\texttt{anatomical\_angles.terms.wrist\_term\_specs}, the description used by
the golf-term registry; no second term list exists.  Angles are in degrees
and times in seconds.  A NaN sample, such as a gimbal-locked component, stays
missing: \texttt{null} in JSON and an empty cell in CSV, with its reason.  Every
row carries \texttt{observability}, the receipt path and both calibration ids.

\paragraph{Exports.}
\texttt{to\_json} writes strict JSON (no NaN) that matches
\texttt{EVENT\_TABLE\_JSON\_SCHEMA} (JSON Schema 2020-12, schema id
\texttt{upstreamdrift.anatomical\_event\_table}, version 1).  \texttt{to\_csv}
writes \texttt{\#} header lines with the units, handedness, event source,
reference, and each term's frame and sign convention, followed by one row
per event and term.

\paragraph{Verification and Open Items.}
\path{tests/unit/anatomical_angles/test_event_table.py} uses synthetic series
with known values.  It checks the values at each event for both handedness
choices, the provenance on every row, the reasons for missing values, the
rejection of invalid events and inputs, schema validation, and the CSV
header.  No best-fit or tour values are reported here.  Open items for
\#13175: pelvis reversal and trunk terms, the API endpoint, the desktop and
web table (\texttt{feature\_parity.json}), and HUD captions in
\texttt{native\_viewer\_export}.
{\small
\begin{verbatim}
py -3 -m pytest -o addopts="" -q -n 0 tests/unit/anatomical_angles/test_event_table.py
\end{verbatim}}

\end{document}
